Preface / Prefásiu

Ho Kódigu Penál ne'ebé moris iha 7 Juñu 2009 Timór-Leste hametin tan nia
sistema-jurídiku.

Maibé, hanesan lei hotu-hotu, ema sira-ne'ebé servisu iha tribunál no sidadaun sira presiza lee no kompreende Kódigu Penál Timór ne'e, liuliu hodi hatene hahalok ne'ebé maka lei konsidera krime no hahalok ne'ebé maka lei la konsidera krime ka iha situasaun ne'ebé maka hahalok ida fó-fatin ba pena no iha situasaun ne'ebé maka hahalok ida la fó-fatin
ba pena.

Hamutuk ho difikuldade sira-ne'ebé ita hasoru kuandu ita aplika Kódigu foun ruma, ita iha difikuldade boot ida-tán: ita seidauk domina ita nia lian ofisiál Tetun no
Portugés.

Versaun Tetun laiha karik, ema sira-ne'ebé ladún hatene Portugés sei iha difikuldade boot atu iha asesu ba kódigu ne'e.

Tanba ne'e maka ha'u hodi-biit tradús Kódigu ida-ne'e
ba Tetun.

With the Penal Code in force from 7 June 2009, Timor-Leste took another step in the consolidation of its legal system.

But, like any law, the Timorese Penal Code needs to be read and understood, not only by those who work in the courts but also by citizens, who need to know when a conduct constitutes a crime or in what situations a certain conduct gives rise to a penalty and in what situations it does not.

The lack of mastery of the official languages – Tetum and Portuguese – is a difficulty to add to those inherent in the application of a new code.

Without a version in the Tetum language this code will be difficult to access for those who do not yet have sufficient knowledge of Portuguese.

For this reason, I took the risk of translating this statute into Tetum.

Ha'u hatene katak halo tradusaun ba textu jurídiku difisil tebes; difisil liu-tán tanba ha'u la'ós tékniku iha tradusaun; difisil liu-tán tanba tradusaun ne'e tenke halo ba lian ne'ebé sei dezenvolve-án daudaun.
Maibé ha'u fiar katak, nune'e, ha'u fo mós korajen ba jurista grupu ki'ikoan ida-ne'ebé, hamutuk ho ha'u, estuda tiha ona ka estuda daudaun Tetun tuir perspetiva jurídika iha Centro de Formação Jurídica atu hakás-án liu-tán hodi dezenvolve ita nia lian Tetun atu sai hanesan lian iha servisu iha Timór-Leste
nia tribunál.

Kona-ba ortografia ha'u hakás-an atu tuir padraun ortográfiku ba lian Tetun ne'ebé Institutu Nasionál ba
Linguístika, iha Universidade Nasionál Timór-Loro Sa'e, dezenvolve, nu'udar Dekretu
1/2004 haruka.

Atu garante nafatin rigór iha konseitu jurídiku ida-idak, ha'u adota hanesan tetun lia-fuan portugés barak, ho laran-metin katak, kuandu ita uza beibeik ona, ita sei toman lia-fuan sira-ne'e. Tanba rigór mós, ha'u la uza termu tetun sira-

I did so with the awareness of the difficulties inherent in the translation of any legal text, done by someone who is not a translation technician and into a language still under development; but also with the certainty that, in doing so, I am encouraging the small group of jurists who were or are with me taking an approach to Tetum from a legal perspective at the Legal Training Centre to commit themselves even more to the development of this national language to make it more easily usable as a working language in the courts of Timor-Leste.

In orthography I was careful to respect the orthographic standard of the Tetum language developed by the National Institute of Linguistics of the National University of Timor-Leste, as imposed by Decree 1/2004.

To maintain the rigour of legal concepts I chose to adopt terms from Portuguese, which use will quickly make familiar. For the same reason I chose not yet to use some Tetum terms whose meaning in legal discourse still

ne'ebé seidauk iha signifikadu metin iha diskursu jurídiku.

Iha publikasaun ne'e hatama mós, ho nota iha rodapé, signifikadu ba liafuan balu ne'ebé uza iha ne'e, hodi fasilita deit sira nia kompreensaun, ho laran metin katak aban-bairua sei tau-hamutuk iha glosáriu ida termu jurídiku sira-ne'ebé uza daudaun iha ne'e.

Livru ida-ne'e tau hamutuk Kódigu Penál Timór, lei ne'ebé fo autorizasaun ba Governu atu aprova kódigu ne'e ho dekretu-lei no mós lei ne'ebé altera kódigu ne'e nia artigu 141, sira hotu iha versaun Portugés no Tetun.

Ha'u agradese ba PNUD nia Programa ba Justisa tanba apoiu hodi publika livru ida-
ne'e. Dili, 13 Outubru 2009

needs to be consolidated.
In this publication an attempt is made to indicate, in footnotes, the meaning of some of the terms used, only to facilitate their understanding, in the expectation that in the future a glossary of the legal terms used here will be organized.

This book contains the Portuguese and Tetum versions of the Timorese Penal Code, of the law that authorizes the Government to approve the code by decree-law, as well as of the law that amended article 141 of the code.

I thank the UNDP Justice Programme for having financed the publication of this book.

Dili, 13 October 2009

Cláudio Ximenes
Tribunál ba Rekursu nia Prezidente / President of the Court of Appeal

Law 13/2008, of 13 October (Legislative authorisation in criminal matters)

Law 6/2009, of 15 July (Amends article 141 of the Penal Code)

Decree-Law 19/2009, of 8 April (Approves the Penal Code)

Given the need to construct the legal system, the drafting and approval of the Penal Code of Timor-Leste was established by political leaders as one of the legislative priorities for guaranteeing the fundamental rights and freedoms enshrined in the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste.

The present legal instrument results from the work developed by a commission of Timorese and international technicians that acted under governmental guidance and in strict observance of the limits and content established in the law of legislative authorisation in criminal matters approved in the National Parliament.
The normative solutions enshrined, in addition to respecting the specific social and cultural realities of the Timorese community, also incorporate suggestions made by national and international organisations, contributions from various judicial operators working in Timor-Leste, as well as lessons drawn from comparative law.

It should be emphasised that the Penal Code now approved, more than a definitive point of arrival, constitutes rather a fundamental stage in the construction of the Timorese legal order, always open to future improvements that, in the future, the evolution of international law, judicial practice and the teaching of law may advise.

Thus,

In the exercise of the legislative authorisation granted under articles 1 and 2 of Law no. 13/2008, of 13 October and under the terms provided for in article 96 of the Constitution, the Government decrees, to have force of law, the following:

Article 1 Approval of the Penal Code

The Penal Code published in the annex and which forms an integral part of the present instrument is approved.

Article 2 Repealing provision

1.	The Indonesian Penal Code, in force in the legal order under the terms provided for in art.1 of Law 10/2003, is repealed.
2.	All legal provisions contained in separate legislation that are repealed:
a)	Provide for and punish acts criminalised by the Penal Code now approved;
b)	Establish solutions contrary to those adopted in the General Part of the Penal Code.

Article 3 Entry into force

The present instrument and the Penal Code enter into force on the 60th day following their publication.

Approved in Council of Ministers on 18 March 2009.

The Prime Minister,
(Kay Rala Xanana Gusmão)

The Minister of Justice, (Lúcia M. B. F. Lobato)

Promulgated on 30/03/09 Publish.

The President of the Republic, (José Ramos Horta)

Annex

PENAL CODE

I	– The restoration of independence and the approval of the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste in 2002, determined the need for the country to adopt its own modern legal order, which would enshrine the fundamental rights set out in the constitutional precepts and reflect the social reality of the country. The maintenance in force of the Indonesian Penal Code was necessary to ensure the validity of a criminal law of the State, but proved inadequate to the new reality of the country and in various circumstances, determined legal solutions contrary to the constitutional principles now enshrined.

Since the Timorese people have their own specificity and identity, the need arose to draft their own Penal Code, with a philosophy that was intrinsic to them and guided by principles and values inherent to modern societies, which would respond to the current demands placed on the Country.

A commission of Timorese and international technicians, created by the I Constitutional Government, proceeded to draft a Draft Penal Code which, despite the approval of the Law of Legislative Authorisation in criminal matters, was not promulgated at the end of the legislature

At the beginning of 2008, with a new executive, a new proposal for a Law of legislative authorisation for the approval of the Penal Code is presented to the National Parliament and the work of revising the draft Penal Code is resumed, with the instrument having been subject to amendments and subjected to a broad public discussion.

After the approval of the legislative authorisation, it fell to the Council of Ministers of the IV Constitutional Government to approve the Penal Code.

II	– The General Part constitutes Book I of the Penal Code, and integrates the fundamental principles of criminal law enshrined in the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste in the conventions, treaties and international agreements received in the Timorese internal legal order.

Because it is a code based on a Democratic Rule of Law, the General Part enshrines the principle of human dignity, respect for the individual freedom of each citizen and the responsibility of the State to intervene only when there are unsustainable injuries to legal interests fundamental to life in society, and the State must, in these circumstances, assume the right to punish and the social duty to reintegrate the agent into society.

Also reflecting a Rule of Law are the principle of legality, the principle of guilt and the principle of humanity.

The enshrinement of the principle of legality, as a fundamental principle of Criminal Law, provided for in article 31 of the Constitution, determines that any action or omission can only be considered a crime and be punished as such, when provided for in the law. Observance of this principle requires the prohibition of the application of analogy in matters of qualification of crimes, and the Court cannot through an analogical interpretation of the norms contained in the Penal Code qualify a fact as a crime, define a state of dangerousness or determine a penalty or security measure.

The principle of non-retroactivity of criminal law, which prevents the retroactive application of criminal law, except when it proves concretely more favourable to the defendant, is also a corollary of the principle of legality.

The principle of humanity, enshrined in articles 29 and 32 of the Constitution, is the guiding principle that underlies the prohibition of the application of the death penalty, as well as the determination of penalties or security measures of a perpetual nature or of unlimited or indefinite duration.

Necessity, proportionality and adequacy are guiding principles of the normative options that inform the present Code and underlie the application of each penalty or security measure, these always having as their purpose the protection of legal interests essential to life in society and the social reintegration of the agent.

The principle of guilt is observed as a prerequisite for the application of the penalty, determining that there is no penalty without guilt (nulla poena sine culpa), this principle being a form of limitation of the power of the State, since the measure of the penalty can never exceed the measure of guilt. The principle of guilt is also reflected in the treatment given to error regarding unlawfulness, in the enshrinement of non-imputability by reason of age and in the verification of psychic anomaly, which exclude the criminal responsibility of the agent for lack of guilt.

Regarding the legal consequences of the punishable act, it is observed that the penalty must always be executed in a pedagogical and resocialising manner, with this Code presenting different means of applying non-institutional sanctioning measures.

When, as an alternative, a custodial or non-custodial penalty must be applied, the court is required to give reasoned preference to the non-custodial penalty whenever it adequately and sufficiently achieves the purposes of punishment and satisfies the requirements of reprobation and prevention of crime (

artigo 62º)

Thus, the application of alternative penalties is prioritized, especially in situations of minor and medium-level crime, with particular emphasis on the penalty of fine and the penalty of community service, which are enshrined as ways to better ensure the intended social reintegration of the offender.

The fine penalty is set in days, thus allowing better adaptation to the agent's guilt and their economic conditions, with the amount set for each day of fine varying according to the economic and financial situation of the convicted person and their personal obligations.

On the other hand, in order to differentiate the fine penalty, as a principal penalty, from the fine substituting the prison sentence, different rules are established for converting the fine into days of imprisonment, in case of non-payment of the fine.

Regarding community service, as a non-institutional sanctioning measure, the Code is concerned with clarifying and systematizing some fundamental aspects of its regime, leaving its development and concrete application to autonomous law.

The prison sentence should only be applied when all other penalties prove inadequate to achieve the objectives of prevention and reprobation. A minimum duration of 30 days and a maximum of 25 years is established for the prison sentence, and the maximum limit may be increased to 30 years in cases specially provided for by law.

A corollary of the offender's social reintegration is the institute of suspension of execution of the prison sentence, applicable in cases where the measure of the sentence in concrete terms does not exceed 3 years and the requirements of prevention of future crimes do not prevent its enforcement. The suspension of execution of the prison sentence may be conditioned on compliance with duties or rules of conduct, or subject to monitoring by reintegration services.

For those not criminally responsible by virtue of mental abnormality, security measures of limited temporal duration are established, namely the measure of confinement, whenever reasons of dangerousness so advise. The application of measures of a perpetual nature is not permitted, and the obligation to cease security measures is established whenever the state of dangerousness that legitimized them ends, admitting, in the case of foreigners, that the measures may be replaced by expulsion from the country.

The Penal Code, in defense of the fundamental values and legal interests for life in society, distinguishes crimes of a public nature, which must necessarily be protected by the State, from those which, being less serious, are dependent on the exercise of the right to file a complaint by the holder of that right, as already enshrined in criminal procedural legislation. Thus, all those crimes are considered semi-public which, in the Special Part of the Penal Code, determine the obligation to exercise the complaint in the description of the respective legal type.

Regarding the extinction of criminal responsibility and its effects, the limitation periods for criminal proceedings, penalties, security measures and accessory penalties are set in the general part, as well as situations of suspension are defined. However, the imprescriptibility of criminal proceedings and penalties was chosen when they concern war crimes, crimes against peace, or crimes against humanity and freedom.

Finally, other cases of extinction of responsibility are regulated, such as the death of the agent, amnesty, and pardon.

III – It is recognized that the Special Part of Penal Codes is the one that generates the greatest impact on public opinion, insofar as it translates into the selection of goods, interests and values that in a given society and at a given historical moment justify being protected by criminal law, being, consequently, elevated to the category of

criminal legal interests. In the specific case of the Penal Code of Timor-Leste, the legislator sought to embody in the normative solutions found the choices that the Constitution had previously already enshrined as being the collective sentiment of Timorese society.

The systematization adopted in this part follows the history of the country and reflects the fundamental interests and values that built this young nation.

It is not surprising, therefore, that the first title of this book protects, respectively, peace, humanity and freedom as primordial values of democratic societies, in respect for the hierarchy of values that the Fundamental Law indicates and taking into account the international obligations assumed by the Country by subscribing to and ratifying the Statute of the International Criminal Court.

Title II enshrines crimes against persons, with particular protection of eminently personal legal interests, highlighting the protection of life, physical integrity, personal freedom, sexual freedom, and the protection of private life.

It should be noted that voluntary termination of pregnancy is a punishable crime under the terms of article 145 of this Code.

In this title and with regard to crimes against physical integrity, the introduction of crimes of ill-treatment of minors and ill-treatment of spouse assumes particular relevance, fundamental typifications for the affirmation of the Rule of Law and for the protection of human rights in Timorese society.

The criminalization of slavery and trafficking in persons is also highlighted, the fruit of the humanist conception that presided over the elaboration of this Code.

Title III provides for crimes against life in democracy, highlighting crimes against public tranquility, State security and life in

society, as well as electoral crimes and crimes against public authority.

In this title, and within the scope of crimes against life in society, the provision of specific types of crimes against the environment stands out, reflecting a growing concern of society in the preservation of natural resources and the protection of the environment, punishing unsustainably harmful conduct against fauna, flora, and natural habitats.

The protection of property is enshrined in Title IV of this Book, where a normative system is constructed based on the legal types of crime most common in various criminal legislations such as theft, robbery, breach of trust and damage. These types are defined as simple or aggravated, considering the circumstances of value, the nature of the thing appropriated, the means and forms of action, violence, as well as any other circumstances likely to significantly increase the guilt or unlawfulness of the agent.

Crimes against the administration of Justice and crimes committed in the exercise of public functions are listed in Titles V and VI, which enshrine the punishment of falsity of procedural acts, forms of obstruction of jurisdictional activity, its non-exercise as denial of justice, as well as identifying, among others, acts of bribery, prevarication by magistrate or official and by lawyer or public defender. Other activities of personal favoritism in the field of justice and the classic incriminations of false accusation, simulation of crime and failure to report a crime are also criminalized.

In the exercise of public functions, in general, the conduct of officials that subsumes crimes of corruption, embezzlement, abuse of power or public force, or economic participation in business by those who hold public offices or functions is criminalized. In this domain, the Penal Code expands the concept of

public official to include other analogous situations such as an official of international organizations, a foreign public official who exercises activity in the country, or a person who has been called to perform or participate in the performance of an activity included in the public administrative or jurisdictional function.

Title VII typifies crimes of falsification of documents, technical notations, currency and sealed or stamped securities, weights and measures, marks, stamps and seals, diversifying the respective punishments according to the nature, probative or fiduciary value and public use or destination of the falsified objects, providing for the possibility of seizure and forfeiture of objects intended for their practice.

Finally, in Title VIII, crimes against the economy are defined, proceeding to the incrimination of money laundering, in line with the most recent doctrine on the criminalization of anti-economic activities, tax fraud and situations of smuggling and customs evasion, with regard to customs or border issues. The criminal punishment of disobedience to the requisition of goods ordered by the Government is maintained, as well as behaviors likely to disturb, harm or prevent the performance of certain public acts such as public tender or judicial auction.

It should be noted that, throughout this Diploma, an attempt is made to find a balance for abstract criminal frameworks, taking into account the type of crime, its seriousness, the hierarchy of legally protected interests by each of the norms and the maximum limit enshrined for the prison sentence.

Also characteristic of the legislative options adopted by the Penal Code is the different treatment given to more serious crime where, in general, the legal type of crime provides only for a prison sentence.

In minor and medium-level crime, as a rule, the possibility for the court, depending on the cases, to resort, as an alternative, to the penalty of deprivation of liberty or the fine penalty follows directly from the provision of the norm, enshrining the criminal policy orientation of recognizing the fine as an autonomous penalty instead of complementary to the principal penalty.

The approval of this Code provides the Timorese State with yet another legal instrument, modern and adequate for the provision of a public-criminal service of greater quality and respect for the fundamental rights of citizens, requiring continuous training of magistrates, public defenders, lawyers, court officials and other judicial actors who in their daily lives make these legal diplomas their working instruments, strengthening the national legal order and the Democratic Rule of Law.

BOOK I GENERAL PART

TITLE I

APPLICATION OF CRIMINAL LAW

CHAPTER ONE GENERAL PRINCIPLES

Article 1 Principle of legality

1.	No action or omission may be classified as a crime without a law prior to its commission defining it as a crime and imposing the respective penalty.
2.	Security measures may only be applied to states of dangerousness whose prerequisites are defined in prior law.

Article 2 Prohibition of analogy

To classify an action or omission as a crime, define a state of dangerousness or determine the respective legal consequences, recourse to analogy is not permitted.

Article 3

Application of criminal law in time

1.	No one may be punished for an act defined as a crime at the time of its commission if a subsequent law ceases to consider it a crime.
2.	In this case, if there has already been a conviction decision, even if final, the execution and its criminal effects cease.
3.	The law subsequent to the commission of the crime applies to prior conduct whenever it proves concretely more favourable to the agent and, in cases of final decisions, if any advantage can still be derived.

Article 4

Exceptional or temporary law

Exceptional or temporary law continues to apply to acts committed during its validity even if the period of duration has elapsed or the circumstances that determined it have ceased.

Article 5 Moment of commission of the act

The act is considered committed at the moment of the action or omission, regardless of the moment when the typical result occurs.

Article 6

Place of commission of the act

The act is considered committed in the place where, in any form, the action or omission occurred, in whole or

in part, as well as where the typical result was produced or should have been produced.

Article 7 Principle of territoriality

Unless an international treaty or convention provides otherwise and whatever the nationality of the agent, Timorese criminal law is applicable to acts committed in the territory of Timor-Leste and aboard ships or aircraft registered or flying the Timorese flag.

Article 8

Acts committed outside the national territory

Unless a treaty or convention provides otherwise, Timorese criminal law is applicable to acts committed outside the territory of Timor-Leste in the following cases:
a)	When they constitute the crimes provided for in articles 196 to 206, 229 to 242 and 307 to 313;
b)	When they constitute the crimes provided for in articles 123 to 135, 161 to 169 and 175 to 178 provided that the agent is found in Timor-Leste and cannot be extradited or a decision is made not to surrender him;
c)	When they are committed against Timorese provided that the agent habitually lives in Timor-Leste and is found here;
d)	When they are committed by Timorese or by foreigners against Timorese, provided that the agent is found in Timor-Leste, the acts are equally punishable by the legislation of the place where they were committed and constitute a crime that admits extradition and extradition cannot, in the case, be granted;
e)	If they are crimes that the Timorese State, by international convention or treaty, has undertaken to try.

Article 9

Restrictions on the application of Timorese law

1.	Timorese criminal law is only applicable to acts committed outside the national territory when the agent has not been tried with a final decision in the place of commission of the act or, having been tried, evades total or partial compliance with the sanction.
2.	Even if, under the terms of the preceding number, Timorese criminal law is applicable, the act is tried according to the law of the country in which it was committed if this is concretely more favourable to the agent.
3.	In the cases referred to in the preceding number, the applicable sanction is converted into the one corresponding to it in the Timorese criminal system or, if there is no correspondence, into the one that Timorese law provides for the act.
4.	In the case that the agent is tried in Timor-Leste, having been tried previously in the place of commission of the act, account is taken of the penalty that has already been served abroad.
5.	The regime provided for in number 2 does not apply to the crimes identified in paragraphs a) and b) of the preceding article.

Article 10 Subsidiary application

Unless otherwise provided, the norms of this Code are applicable to acts punishable by special legislation.

TITLE II OF CRIME

CHAPTER I GENERAL PREREQUISITES

Article 11 Action and omission

1.	When a legal type of crime comprises a certain result, the act encompasses not only the action adequate to produce it but also the omission of the action adequate to avoid it, unless the intention of the law is otherwise.
2.	The commission of a result by omission is only punishable when a legal duty rests upon the omitter that personally obliges him to avoid that result.

3.	In the case provided for in the preceding number, the penalty may be extraordinarily mitigated.

Article 12 Criminal liability

1.	Criminal liability for the offences provided for in this Code falls to natural persons and is non-transferable.
2.	Legal persons are only criminally liable for the offences provided for in this Code or in special legislation when and under the conditions that the law expressly establishes.

Article 13

Liability for acting on behalf of another

Whoever acts as holder of an organ of a legal person, or mere de facto association, or as representative of another, is punishable even if the conditions, qualities or relations provided for in the respective type of crime are not verified in him, but rather in the person of the represented party.

Article 14 Subjective imputation

Only an act committed with intent, or in cases specially provided for by law, with negligence, is punishable.

Article 15 Types of intent

1.	Acts with intent whoever, representing an act that fulfils a type of crime, acts with the intention of carrying it out.
2.	Also acts with intent whoever represents the realization of an act that fulfils a type of crime as a necessary consequence of his conduct.
3.	When the realization of an act that fulfils a type of crime is represented as a possible consequence of the

conduct, there is intent if the agent acts conforming to that realization.

Article 16 Types of negligence

1.	Acts with negligence whoever, by not proceeding with the care to which, according to the circumstances, he is obliged and capable:
a)	Represents as possible the realization of an act that fulfils a type of crime but acts without conforming to that realization; or
b)	Does not even come to represent the possibility of realization of the act.
2.	The types of negligence referred to in the preceding number take the form of gross negligence whenever the circumstances show that the agent acted with recklessness or temerity, not observing the elementary duties of prudence required in the case.

Article 17

Error as to circumstances

1.	Error as to the elements of fact or law of a legal type of crime, as well as error as to prohibitions whose knowledge is reasonably indispensable for the agent to become aware of the unlawfulness, excludes intent.
2.	The regime provided for in the preceding number includes error as to the existence of the prerequisites of a cause of exclusion of unlawfulness or culpability.
3.	The punishability of negligent conduct is reserved whenever the law provides for it and the respective prerequisites are verified.

Article 18

Error as to unlawfulness

1.	Ignorance of the law does not exclude the unlawfulness of the conduct that violates it.

2.	Error as to the unlawfulness of an act, if unavoidable, excludes culpability.
3.	If the error as to unlawfulness is avoidable, the penalty may be extraordinarily mitigated.

Artigo 19º Agravação pelo resultado

When the penalty applicable to an act is aggravated by reason of the production of a result, the aggravation is always conditioned upon the possibility of imputing that result to the agent at least on grounds of negligence.

Artigo 20º Inimputabilidade em razão da idade

1.	Minors under 16 years of age are criminally non-imputable.
2.	To young persons over 16 and under 21 years of age, the provisions of this statute apply in all matters not covered by autonomous legislation, with respect to the application and execution of criminal sanctions.

Artigo 21º

Inimputabilidade em razão de anomalia psíquica

1.	A person is non-imputable who, by reason of a psychic anomaly, is incapable, at the time of commission of the act, of evaluating the unlawfulness thereof or of determining himself in accordance with that evaluation.
2.	A person may be declared non-imputable who, by reason of a psychic anomaly, has, at the time of commission of the act, appreciably diminished capacity to evaluate the unlawfulness or to determine himself in accordance with that evaluation.
3.	The proven incapacity of the agent to be influenced by penalties may constitute an indication of the situation provided for in the preceding paragraph.

4.	Imputability is not excluded when the psychic anomaly has been provoked by the agent with the intention of committing the act.

CAPÍTULO II FORMAS DO CRIME

Artigo 22º Actos preparatórios

Preparatory acts are not punishable, except where legal provision provides otherwise.

Artigo 23º Tentativa

There is an attempt when the agent initiates the execution of a crime he has decided to commit, performing part or all of the acts objectively adequate to produce the result and the result does not occur by reason of circumstances beyond his will.

Artigo 24º Punibilidade da tentativa

1.	An attempt is punishable only in intentional crimes to which corresponds a penalty of imprisonment whose maximum limit exceeds 3 years and in other cases that the law expressly determines.
2.	Except where otherwise provided, an attempt is punishable with the penalty corresponding to the consummated crime extraordinarily mitigated.

Artigo 25º Tentativa não punível

An attempt is not punishable when the means employed by the agent is manifestly inadequate or the object essential to the consummation of the crime is non-existent.

Artigo 26º

Desistência voluntária
An attempt by a person who voluntarily desists from continuing the execution of the crime, prevents the consummation or occurrence of the result, or makes a serious effort to prevent one or the other, is not punishable.

Artigo 27º

Casos de comparticipação

In case of participation, an attempt by a person who voluntarily desists from continuing the execution of the crime, prevents the consummation or occurrence of the result, or makes a serious effort to prevent one or the other, is not punishable, even if the other participants continue the execution of the act or consummate it.

Artigo 28º Arrependimento posterior

In crimes without violence or serious threat against persons, where the damage is repaired, the thing restored or the situation legalized before a report is made or a denunciation or complaint is received, the penalty may be extraordinarily mitigated or, depending on the circumstances, the agent exempted from penalty.

CAPÍTULO III AGENTES DO CRIME

Artigo 29º Agentes

Participation in the commission of a crime may take the form of authorship, instigation or complicity and there may be several participants in the same act.

Artigo 30º Autoria

1.	An author is one who executes the act, by himself or through another, whom he uses as an instrument.
2.	They are co-authors if, by tacit or express agreement, they take direct part in the execution or act in conjunction of efforts for the commission of the same crime.

Artigo 31º Instigação

A person who directly and intentionally determines another to commit a crime is punishable as an author, provided there is execution or commencement of execution of the crime.

Artigo 32º Cumplicidade

1.	A person who intentionally assists another materially or morally to commit a crime is punishable as an accomplice.
2.	The penalty corresponding to the type of unlawful act, extraordinarily mitigated, is applicable to the accomplice.

Artigo 33º

Culpa na comparticipação

Each participant is punished according to his culpability, independently of the punishment or degree of culpability of the others.

Artigo 34º

Ilicitude na comparticipação

1.	If the unlawfulness or degree of unlawfulness of the act depends on certain special qualities or relations of the agent, it suffices that those qualities or relations exist in any one of them, to make the respective penalty applicable to all participants, except if the intention of the incriminating norm is otherwise.
2.	Whenever, by effect of the rule provided for in the preceding paragraph, the application of a more severe penalty results for any of the participants, this may, considering the

circumstances of the case, be replaced by that which would apply if such rule did not intervene.

CAPÍTULO IV CONCURSOS E CRIMES CONTINUADOS

Artigo 35º Concurso de crimes

1.	The number of crimes is determined by the number of types of crimes actually committed, or by the number of times the same type is fulfilled by the conduct of the agent.
2.	For the purpose of the provisions of the following article, the case in which the agent, having committed a crime, commits another before having been convicted by a final judgment, is considered as a concurrence.

Artigo 36º

Punição em caso de concurso

1.	In case of concurrence of crimes, a single penalty is applied, whose minimum limit corresponds to the highest of the penalties concretely applied to the various crimes and the maximum limit to the material sum of the various partial penalties.
2.	The maximum limit of the framework of the concurrence may not exceed 600 days for a fine or 30 years for imprisonment, even in cases where the material sum of the partial penalties exceeds that legal limit.
3.	In determining the single penalty the court considers, together, the facts and the personality of the agent.

Artigo 37º Concurso de sanções

1.	If the penalties applied are some of fine and others of imprisonment, the different nature of these is maintained.
2.	Accessory penalties and security measures, even if provided for by only one of the applicable laws or in only one of the previous decisions, are maintained.

Artigo 38º

Pena de prisão com execução suspensa em cúmulo jurídico

A penalty of imprisonment with suspended execution may only be legally cumulated with other penalties of imprisonment when:
a)	They are also penalties of imprisonment suspended in their execution and the said cumulation does not prevent the continuation of the regime of suspension of the single penalty;
b)	In the case of cumulation with effective penalties of imprisonment, there exist circumstances that determine the revocation of the suspension of execution of the penalty, independently of the cumulation of penalties;
c)	The suspended penalties to be cumulated have different periods of suspension or, being equal, are in different phases of compliance and the court establishes a single period of suspension in accordance with the needs of prevention and the circumstances of the case.

Artigo 39º

Conhecimento superveniente do concurso

If, after a final judgment, but before the respective penalty is served, prescribed or extinguished, it becomes known that the agent was in one of the situations described in the preceding articles, the rules enshrined therein apply.

Artigo 40º Crime e outra infracção

If the same act simultaneously constitutes a crime and an administrative offence, the agent is punished as a crime, without prejudice to the application of the accessory sanctions provided for the other infractions.

Article 41 Continuing crime

1. Except in the case of crimes that protect eminently personal interests, the multiple commission of the same type of crime or of various types of crime that fundamentally protect the same legal interest constitutes a single continuing crime, executed in an essentially homogeneous manner and within the framework of solicitation by the same external situation that considerably diminishes the guilt of the agent.
2. The continuing crime is punishable with the penalty applicable to the most serious conduct that forms part of the continuation.

Article 42 Concurrence of norms

To a fact capable of being qualified as a crime, in whole or in part, by more than one legal provision, not being the situations described in the previous articles, only one incriminating norm is applied according to the following rules:
a) The special norm applies to the detriment of the general norm;
b) The subsidiary norm prevails over the principal norm;
c) The broader and more complex norm consumes that which provides for facts subsumable within its provision.

CHAPTER V CAUSES OF EXCLUSION

SECTION I

CAUSES OF EXCLUSION OF UNLAWFULNESS

Article 43 Exclusion of unlawfulness

1. The fact is not criminally punishable when its unlawfulness is excluded by the legal order considered in its totality.
2. In particular, a fact committed in the exercise of a right or in the performance of a duty, in legitimate defence, in a justifying state of necessity or with consent is not unlawful.

Article 44 Legitimate defence

The action of the agent necessary to repel an unlawful, imminent or current aggression against any legally protected interests of the agent or of a third party is considered legitimate defence.

Article 45 State of necessity

A fact committed as an adequate means to avert a current danger that threatens legally protected interests of the agent or of a third party is not unlawful, when the following requirements are met:
a) There is a significant superiority of the interest to be safeguarded in relation to the interest sacrificed; and
b) It is reasonable to impose on the injured party the sacrifice of his interest having regard to the nature or value of the interest in danger.

Article 46 Conflict of duties

1. The conduct of a person who, in case of conflict in the performance of legal duties or legitimate orders of authority, satisfies a duty or order of equal or superior value to that of the duty or order that he sacrifices is not unlawful.
2. The duty of hierarchical obedience ceases when it leads to the commission of a crime.

Article 47 Consent

1. In addition to the cases specially provided for by law, consent excludes the unlawfulness of the fact when it refers to freely available legal interests and the fact does not offend good morals.
2. Consent may be expressed by any means that reflects a serious, free and informed will of the

holder of the legally protected interest, and may be freely revoked until the execution of the fact.
3. Consent is only effective if given by a person over 16 years of age who possesses the necessary discernment to assess its meaning and scope at the time it is given.
4. If consent is not known to the agent, the agent is punishable with the penalty applicable to an attempt.
5. Actual consent is equivalent to presumed consent, which exists when the situation in which the agent acts reasonably allows it to be supposed that the holder of the legally protected interest would have effectively consented to the fact, had he known the circumstances in which it is committed.

SECTION II

CAUSES OF EXCLUSION OF GUILT

Article 48 Excess of legitimate defence

1. The means that, by their type or degree of use, are excessive for the defensive action of the agent may determine the extraordinary mitigation of the penalty that would apply to the crime.
2. The excess of means used in legitimate defence due to disturbance, fear or fright, not blameworthy, excludes the guilt of the agent.

Article 49

Exculpatory state of necessity

1. A person who commits an unlawful act adequate to avert a current danger, not otherwise removable, that threatens the life, physical integrity, honour or freedom of the agent or of a third party, acts without guilt when it is not reasonable to demand of him, according to the circumstances of the case, different behaviour.
2. If the danger threatens legal interests different from those referred to in the previous number, and the remaining presuppositions mentioned there are met, the penalty may be

extraordinarily mitigated or, exceptionally, the agent may be exempted from the penalty.

Article 50 Exculpatory undue obedience

A public official who complies with an order without knowing that it leads to the commission of a crime acts without guilt, this not being evident within the framework of the circumstances represented by him.

TITLE III

OF CIRCUMSTANCES

SOLE CHAPTER GENERAL RULES

Article 51 Determination of the measure of the penalty

1. The determination of the measure of the penalty, within the limits defined by law, is made according to the guilt of the agent and the requirements of prevention.
2. In the concrete determination of the penalty the court takes into account all the circumstances that, not forming part of the type of crime, speak in favour of the agent or against him.
3. In the judgment the grounds for the measure of the penalty are expressly stated.

Article 52 General aggravating circumstances

1.	All circumstances prior to, contemporaneous with or subsequent to the crime are considered general aggravating circumstances of the agent's liability which, not forming part of the legal type, reveal a greater degree of unlawfulness of the facts, of the conduct or of the agent's guilt, increasing the need for punishment.
2.	The following are capable of constituting general aggravating circumstances, among others:

a)	Execution of the crime with disloyalty, it being understood that this occurs in cases of betrayal, ambush, lying in wait, disguise
b)	The crime being committed against persons employing means, modes or forms that directly or indirectly aim to guarantee execution without the danger that could result from the possible defence of the victim;
c)	The crime being committed by means of fraud, deceit, abuse of power or authority or taking advantage of the circumstances of place, time
d)	The crime being committed by means of payment or receipt of a value or reward;
e)	The crime being motivated by racist reasons or any other discriminatory sentiment because of the victim's gender, ideology, religion or beliefs, ethnicity, nationality to which they belong, by reason of sex or sexual orientations, or illness or physical impairment from which they suffer;
f)	The agent having a special duty not to commit the crime, to prevent its commission or to contribute to its punishment or taking advantage, for the commission of the crime, of public authority that they hold or invoke;
g)	Not being a case of recidivism, the agent having committed one or more crimes of the same nature in the 3 years prior to the moment of commission of that for which they are being tried, regardless of the time of conviction;
h)	The crime being committed on the same occasion as another crime as a means of facilitating the commission of another or other crimes;
i)	Execution of the crime being facilitated by the fact that the agent enters or attempts to enter the victim's residence or uses poison, flooding, fire, explosion, shipwreck or boat breakdown or weapon
j)	The commission of the crime or the exploitation of its consequences being facilitated by the participation of two or more persons;

k)	Intentionally and inhumanly increasing the victim's suffering by causing them suffering unnecessary to the consummation or any other acts of spoliation, cruelty or destruction also unnecessary to the execution of the crime;
l)	The victim being or having been a spouse or being in a de facto situation identical thereto, or being an ascendant, descendant, sibling, adoptee or adopter of the agent;
m)	The victim being a particularly vulnerable person by reason of age, illness or physical or mental disability, whenever such circumstance does not form part of the legal type.

Article 53 Recidivism

1.	A person is punished as a recidivist who, alone or under any form of co-participation, commits an intentional crime that must be punished with effective imprisonment exceeding
6 months, after having been convicted by final judgment to a sentence of effective imprisonment exceeding 6 months for another intentional crime, if, according to the circumstances of the case, the agent is to be blamed for the previous conviction or convictions not having served as sufficient warning against crime.
2.	If more than four years have elapsed between the commission of one crime and the other, recidivism does not occur, the time during which the agent served a procedural measure, sentence or security measure depriving liberty not being counted in said period.
3.	In case of recidivism, the minimum limit of the penalty applicable to the crime is raised by one third and the maximum limit remains unchanged, the aggravation not being able to exceed the measure of the most serious penalty applied in previous convictions.

Article 54 Criminal habituality

1.	When the agent commits an intentional crime to which, concretely, a sentence of effective imprisonment exceeding 1 year must be applied, the following requirements occurring cumulatively:
a)	The agent having previously committed three or more intentional crimes to which a sentence of effective imprisonment has been applied;
b)	Less than three years having elapsed between each of the crimes and the following one;
c)	The joint assessment of the facts and the agent's personality revealing a marked or dangerous tendency towards crime;
the applicable penalty is that of the crime committed, the minimum and maximum limits being raised by one third.
2.	The provisions of this precept prevail over the specific rules for the punishment of recidivism.

Article 55 General mitigating circumstances

1.	General mitigating circumstances of the agent's liability are considered to be circumstances that precede, accompany or occur after the commission of the crime and that testify in favour of the agent.
2.	The following are capable of constituting general mitigating circumstances, among others:
a)	The grounds for exclusion referred to in the previous chapter, whenever the totality of the requirements provided for the ground for exclusion to produce effects is not verified;
b)	The agent acting as a consequence of facts causing violent emotion, obsession or other passional state of identical nature or reacting in immediate act to provocation;
c)	The agent presenting themselves voluntarily to the authorities before knowing of the existence of criminal proceedings against them;
d)	The agent spontaneously confessing the commission of the crime or contributing decisively to the ascertainment of the circumstances in which the criminal action occurred;

e)	The	existence	of	acts	demonstrating	the agent's sincere repentance;
f)	The low intensity of intent or negligence;
g)	Reconciliation between the victim and the agent.

Article 56

Circumstances of extraordinary mitigation

1.	In addition to the cases expressly provided for by law, the penalty provided for in the legal type of crime is extraordinarily mitigated whenever there exist circumstances prior to, contemporaneous with or subsequent to the crime that jointly or individually, diminish in a marked manner the unlawfulness of the conduct, the agent's guilt or the need for punishment:
2.	For the purposes of the provisions of the preceding number, the following circumstances are considered, among others:
a)	The agent having acted under the influence of serious threat or under the ascendancy of a person on whom they depend or to whom they owe obedience;
b)	The agent's conduct being determined by honourable motive, by strong solicitation or temptation of the victim themselves or by unjust provocation or undeserved offence;
c)	The agent repairing the damage caused or diminishing its effects, at any moment of the proceedings but before the trial hearing has begun;
d)	A long time having elapsed since the commission of the crime, the agent maintaining good conduct;
e)	The agent being a bearer of sensibly diminished imputability.

Article 57

Degrees of extraordinary mitigation
1.	Whenever there is occasion for extraordinary mitigation of the penalty, the following is observed with respect to the limits of the applicable penalty:
a)	Maximum limit of the prison sentence is reduced by one third;

b)	Minimum limit of the prison sentence is reduced to one fifth if it is equal to or greater than 3 years and to the legal minimum if it is less;
c)	Maximum limit of the fine is reduced by one third and the minimum limit reduced to the legal minimum;
d)	If the maximum limit of the prison sentence is not greater than 3 years it may be replaced by a fine, within the general limits.
2.	The extraordinarily mitigated penalty that has been fixed in concreto is capable of substitution, including suspension, under the general terms.

Article 58 Concurrence of circumstances

1.	The occurrence of one circumstance modifying the abstract framework of the legal type determines the application of the extraordinary mitigation regime provided for in the preceding article.
2.	In the case of two or more circumstances modifying the abstract framework of the legal type concurring, only one is considered under the terms of the preceding article, the remaining ones functioning as circumstances of a general character in the determination of the measure of the penalty.

TITLE IV

LEGAL CONSEQUENCES OF CRIME

CHAPTER I GENERAL PROVISIONS

Article 59

Penalties and security measures

1. The application of the death penalty or of a custodial sentence or security measure of a perpetual nature or of unlimited or indefinite duration is not permitted.

2. The simultaneous application of a penalty and a security measure depriving liberty for the commission of the same act is not permitted.

Article 60

Limits of penalties and security measures

1. The concrete penalty can never exceed the measure of guilt.
2. The security measure is based on the dangerousness of the perpetrator of an act provided for as a crime and lasts until it is verified that the state of criminal dangerousness that gave rise to it has ceased, and may not have a duration exceeding the maximum limit of the penalty corresponding to the crime referred to.

Article 61

Purpose of penalties and security measures

The application of penalties and security measures aims at the protection of legal interests essential to life in society and the reintegration of the perpetrator into it.

Article 62

Choice of penalty and security measure

1. If custodial and non-custodial penalties are applicable to the crime in the alternative, the court gives preference to the latter whenever it adequately and sufficiently fulfils the purposes of punishment.
2. In choosing the type of security measure to be applied to the perpetrator whose dangerousness is shown to be procedurally established, consideration is given to the personality of the perpetrator and the mode of treatment appropriate to the case.

Article 63

Effects of penalties and security measures No penalty or security measure has, as a necessary effect, the loss of civil, professional or political rights.

Article 64

Execution of custodial penalties or measures

1. The perpetrator sentenced to an effective prison sentence or subject to an internment measure may benefit from the granting of conditional release or release on probation.
2. Unless otherwise provided, after five-sixths of the sentence has been served, the convicted person is mandatorily placed on conditional release.
3. The granting of conditional release requires the consent of the convicted person, except in the circumstances described in the preceding paragraph.
4. The prerequisites and conditions for the granting of conditional release are enshrined in specific legislation, as well as the rights and duties of prisoners and the prerequisites and conditions under which an effective prison sentence may be executed in an open regime.

Article 65

Concurrence of custodial penalties and measures

1. When the same perpetrator has been sentenced to an effective prison sentence and subjected to an internment measure, the latter is executed before the prison sentence and is deducted from it.
2. As soon as the internment measure must cease, the court places the perpetrator on conditional release if the time corresponding to half of the sentence has been served and the release proves compatible with the defence of the legal order and social peace.

CHAPTER II PRISON SENTENCE

Article 66 Duration of prison sentence

1. The prison sentence has a minimum duration of 30 days and a maximum of 25 years.

2. In cases specially provided for by law, the limit of the prison sentence is 30 years.
3. The maximum limit of the sentence referred to in the preceding paragraph can never be exceeded.

Article 67 Substitution of imprisonment by fine

1. A prison sentence applied in a measure not exceeding 1 year is replaced by an equal period of fine, up to the maximum legal limit, whenever the requirements of prevention of future crimes do not require the serving of imprisonment and, in view of the circumstances of the case, the court considers that it should not suspend the execution.
2. Unjustified non-payment of the substitute fine or of any instalment implies the immediate serving of the initially fixed prison sentence, which may be suspended by immediate payment of the entire fine or by the indication of assets as guarantee, always deducting the instalments that have been paid.
3. The court must give reasons for the decision not to effect the substitution whenever the sentence applied permits it.

Article 68

Suspension of execution of imprisonment

1. Whenever the prison sentence applied does not exceed 3 years, the court may suspend its execution for a period to be fixed between 1 and 5 years, counting from the final judgment of the decision.
2. The decision must contain the grounds that determine the suspension, namely, the personality of the perpetrator, the circumstances in which the crime was committed, his previous behaviour, his living conditions and, most especially, the predictability of his future conduct.
3. The court must give reasons for the decision not to effect the suspension whenever the sentence applied permits it.

Article 69

Suspension of imprisonment conditional on duties
1. The court may condition the suspension of execution of the prison sentence on the fulfilment of certain duties, not humiliating, intended to repair the harm of the crime.
2. The following duties, among others, may condition the suspension:
a) Reparation or guarantee of reparation of the damage caused by the crime within a specified period;
b) Public apology to the victim;
c) Performance of certain tasks connected with the crime committed;
d) Delivery of an amount to the State or charitable institution with significance in the reintegration of the convicted person.
3. The duties imposed may not in any case represent obligations for the convicted person whose fulfilment is not reasonably required of him
4. The duties imposed may be modified until the end of the suspension period whenever relevant supervening circumstances occur or of which the court only subsequently becomes aware
5. The provisions of paragraph 2 of the preceding article are correspondingly applicable.

Article 70 Rules of conduct

1. The court may impose on the convicted person the fulfilment, for the duration of the suspension, of rules of conduct intended to facilitate his reintegration into society, namely:
a) Not exercising certain professions;

b) Not frequenting certain environments or places;
c) Not residing in certain places or regions;
d) Not accompanying, lodging or receiving certain persons;
e) Not frequenting certain associations or not participating in certain meetings;

f) Not having in his possession objects capable of facilitating the commission of crimes;
g) Reporting periodically to the court, the social reintegration technician or non-police entities.
2. The court may also, having obtained the prior consent of the convicted person, order his subjection to medical treatment or cure in an appropriate institution.
3. The provisions of paragraphs 3 to 5 of the preceding article are correspondingly applicable.

Article 71

Suspension of imprisonment with supervision

1. When simple or conditional suspension of imprisonment is insufficient to guarantee the rehabilitation of the offender and their distancing from criminal activities, the court may decree suspension subjecting the convicted person to supervision by social reintegration services for the duration of the suspension period, obtaining whenever possible the agreement of the convicted person.
2. It is incumbent upon the social reintegration services, jointly with the Public Prosecutor and the sentencing judge, having heard the convicted person, to prepare the social readaptation plan which, once approved by the court, shall be carried out with the assistance of the said official or reintegration service.
3. The readaptation plan must include all duties to which the convicted person is subject, and the court may impose the duties and rules of conduct referred to in Articles 69 and 70 or other obligations relevant to the readaptation plan, namely:
a) Obtain treatment or admission to appropriate establishments, whenever circumstances so require
b) Respond to summonses from the magistrate responsible for enforcement and from the social reintegration officer
c) Receive visits from the social reintegration officer and inform them about their means of subsistence

d) Inform the social reintegration officer about changes of residence and employment, as well as about any absence from residence exceeding 8 days
e) Obtain prior authorization from the magistrate responsible for enforcement to travel abroad
4. The provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 68 are correspondingly applicable.

Article 72

Modification of the suspension regime

If, during the period of suspension of execution of the prison sentence, the convicted person does not comply with the imposed duties or is tried and convicted for another crime, the court, considering the circumstances, may alter the regime of suspension of execution of the prison sentence initially fixed, modify the duties or solemnly warn the convicted person, depending on the circumstances of the case.

Article 73 Revocation of suspension

1. If, during the period of suspension of execution of the prison sentence, the convicted person is tried and convicted for another crime or deliberately relapses in non-compliance with the imposed rules or duties and modification of the suspension regime is not possible or proves insufficient, the court revokes the suspension.
2. Suspension of execution of the prison sentence is always revoked if, during the respective period, the convicted person commits an intentional crime for which they are punished with an effective prison sentence.
3. Revocation of suspension does not give the convicted person the right to demand restitution of payments made during and because of the suspension.

Article 74 Extinction of the prison sentence

Non-revocation of the suspension of execution of the prison sentence determines the extinction of the sentence and its effects, once the suspension period has elapsed.

CHAPTER III FINE PENALTY

Article 75 Duration of the fine penalty

1. The fine penalty is fixed at a minimum of 10 and a maximum of 360 days, unless otherwise provided by law.
2. Each day of fine corresponds to an amount between half a dollar and 200 US dollars, which the court fixes according to the economic and financial situation of the convicted person and their personal obligations.
3. Whenever the circumstances of the case justify it, the court may authorize payment of the fine within a period of 1 year, or allow payment in instalments, provided that the last instalment does not exceed the limit of 2 years after the date on which the conviction becomes final.

Article 76

Imprisonment alternative to the fine penalty

The decision that directly applies a fine penalty fixes imprisonment as an alternative, for the time corresponding to the fine, reduced by two thirds.

Article 77

Reduction or exemption from the fine penalty

1. If the person convicted to a fine penalty does not comply with the penalty due to circumstances, subsequent to the conviction, that make its compliance impossible or difficult and are not attributable to them, the court may decree reduction or exemption from the penalty.
2. The provisions of the preceding paragraph are applicable to the fine penalty that replaces imprisonment.

CHAPTER IV

COMMUNITY SERVICE PENALTY

Article 78

Community service

1. The community service penalty consists of the gratuitous provision of work in a public body or to other entities that the court deems to be of community interest, provided the consent of the convicted person is obtained.
2. The duration of the work that the convicted person must provide is fixed by the court, replacing each day of imprisonment fixed in the sentence with one hour of work, never exceeding 240 hours.
3. The work may be provided during or outside normal service hours, continuously or not, without exceeding per day what is permitted under the overtime regime and always in such a way that the survival of the convicted person or their family members is not affected.
4. Unjustified non-compliance with the provision of community service entails compliance with the initially applied penalty, less the days of work already provided, in accordance with paragraph 2.

Article 79 Requirements

1. The community service penalty is applicable by the court in substitution of a prison sentence not exceeding 1 year or a fine penalty, whenever it concludes that by this means the purposes of punishment are adequately and sufficiently achieved and, in the case of imprisonment, there are reasons of criminal prevention that advise against suspension of execution or substitution by fine.
2. The application of community service always depends on the consent of the convicted person and, in the case of substitution for a fine, may be decreed in the sentence or in a subsequent order, provided an application is submitted by the convicted person before seizure is ordered

in enforcement proceedings instituted for non-payment of the fine.
3. The provisions of paragraph 3 of Article 67 are correspondingly applicable.

Article 80

Suspension, reduction or exemption from penalty

If the convicted person does not comply with the provision of work due to circumstances, subsequent to its decree, that make its compliance impossible or difficult, not being attributable to them, the regime provided for in Article 77 applies.

Article 81 Complementary legislation

The other conditions for application of the community service penalty and operation of the community service enforcement services are the subject of a special instrument.

CHAPTER V

ADMONITION PENALTY

Article 82 Admonition

If the agent is found guilty of committing a crime to which corresponds in the abstract a prison sentence not exceeding 3 years or a fine, the court may limit itself to admonishing them provided that, cumulatively:
a) The damage caused by the criminal conduct has been repaired;
b) It is a first-time offender;
c) Criminal prevention and rehabilitation of the offender are satisfied with the admonition.

Article 83

Execution of the admonition penalty

The admonition consists of a solemn and appropriate oral reprimand to the convicted person, executed by the court in a public hearing, after the decision to be applied becomes final.

CHAPTER VI ACCESSORY PENALTIES

Article 84 General principle

1. The law may make correspond to certain crimes the prohibition of exercise of certain rights or professions.
2. Accessory penalties are cumulative with each other and may only be applied simultaneously with a principal penalty, and their duration must be fixed according to the measure of guilt.

Artigo 85º

Temporary suspension from the exercise of public functions

1. The court that sentences someone who holds a public office to an effective prison sentence must decree the suspension of the exercise of that function for the period of serving the sentence, if the convicted person is not dismissed on disciplinary grounds.
2. The effects that, in accordance with the respective legislation, accompany the disciplinary sanction of suspension from the exercise of functions are applicable to the convicted person during the suspension period.
3. The provisions of the preceding paragraphs are correspondingly applicable to professions or activities whose exercise depends on a public title, authorization or approval by public authority.

Artigo 86º

Prohibition from the exercise of function
1. The holder of public office, public official or agent of the Administration, who, in the exercise of the activity

for which he was elected or appointed, is sentenced to a prison sentence exceeding 3 years may be prohibited from the exercise of those functions for a period of 2 to 5 years when any of the following circumstances occurs:
a) The act is committed with flagrant and serious abuse of function or with manifest and serious violation of the duties inherent to the function;
b) The agent reveals unworthiness or manifest incapacity for the exercise of the office;
c) The nature of the act implies the loss of the trust necessary for the exercise of the function.
2. The provisions of the preceding paragraph are correspondingly applicable to professions or activities whose exercise depends on a public title or on authorization or approval by public authority.
3. The time during which the convicted person is deprived of liberty as a consequence of a coercive measure, sentence or security measure does not count towards the prohibition period.
4. The accessory penalty provided for in this article does not apply when, for the same acts, the security measure provided for in Article 100 is applied.
5. The application of the provisions of this article requires the communication of the conviction from the court to the public authority on which the official depends.

Artigo 87º Expulsion

1. The foreign citizen convicted of committing a crime corresponding to a prison sentence exceeding 3 years may be expelled from the national territory if he has resided there for less than 15 years:
a) For a period of up to 2 years if resident for more than 10 years;
b) For a period of up to 5 years if resident for more than 5 and less than 10 years;
c) For a period of up to 10 years if resident for less than 5 years.
2. The expulsion referred to in the preceding paragraph applies when in the specific case it is required for reasons of

internal security, public health or to prevent the continuation of criminal activity.
3. The expulsion penalty is executed independently of the total or partial serving of the principal sentence.

Artigo 88º Prohibition from driving

1. Anyone who is punished may be subject to disqualification from driving motor vehicles for a period to be fixed between 3 months and 2 years:
a) For a crime provided for in Articles 207 to 209;
b) For a crime committed with the use of a motorized vehicle and whose execution was facilitated by it in a relevant manner; or
c) For a crime of disobedience committed by refusing to submit to the tests legally established for the detection of driving a motorized vehicle under the effect of alcohol, narcotics, psychotropic substances or products with analogous effects impairing physical, mental or psychological fitness.
2. The provisions of paragraph 3 of Article 86 are correspondingly applicable.

Artigo 89º

Revocation of weapon carrying license

1. In case of conviction for an intentional crime committed with the use of a weapon, the court may decree the revocation of the license to use and carry a weapon, considering the circumstances and the seriousness of the conduct, for a period of 2 to 8 years.
2. The provisions of paragraph 3 of Article 86 are correspondingly applicable.

CAPÍTULO VII DETERMINATION OF SENTENCE

Artigo 90º General principles

1. Whenever the law fixes a sentence, it refers to the crime in its consummated form.
2. The framework of the abstract sentence on which the specific measure of sentence is determined is obtained:
a) By applying to the abstract sentence corresponding to the consummated crime the aggravating circumstances modifying recidivism and criminal habituality, provided for respectively in Articles 53 and 54;
b) Having complied with the provisions of the preceding subparagraph or starting from the abstract sentence corresponding to the consummated crime if there are no modifying circumstances, the situations of extraordinary mitigation are considered if, in the case, they exist.

Artigo 91º Specific determination of sentence

1. Having found the abstract framework of the sentence under the terms of the preceding article, the court assesses all circumstances that, not forming part of the type nor having been valued under the terms of the provisions of the preceding article, aggravate or diminish the responsibility of the convicted person.
2. Based on the valuation of these latter circumstances, the court fixes the exact measure of the sentence that it considers necessary for the protection of legal interests essential to life in society and for the social reintegration of the agent, within the limits of the framework fixed in the type or the limits resulting from the application of the provisions of the preceding article.
3. The measure of the sentence applied to the convicted person may not, under any circumstances, exceed the measure of guilt.

Artigo 92º

Special case of fine sentence

In the case of a fine sentence, the provisions of this chapter are applicable to the determination of the duration of the fine, without prejudice to the provisions of paragraph 2 of Article 75 for the calculation of the amount corresponding to each day of fine.

CAPÍTULO VIII SECURITY MEASURES

SECÇÃO I

INTERNMENT MEASURE

Artigo 93º Prerequisites

When an act described in a legal type of crime is committed by a person not criminally liable under the terms of the provisions of Article 21, he may be ordered to be interned in an appropriate establishment, whenever by virtue of the psychic anomaly, the nature and seriousness of the act committed, the court has founded fear that he will commit other typical acts corresponding to crimes against persons or to crimes of common danger.

Artigo 94º Duration

1. If the act committed by the person not criminally liable is punishable with imprisonment up to 3 years, the internment may not last more than 1 year.
2. If the act committed by the person not criminally liable corresponds to a crime against persons or to a crime of common danger, punishable with a prison sentence equal to or exceeding 5 years, the internment has a minimum duration of 3 years, unless the release proves compatible with the defense of public order and social peace.
3. If the act committed by the person not criminally liable corresponds to a crime punishable with a sentence exceeding 8 years and the danger of new acts of the same kind is so serious as to advise against release, the internment may be extended for successive periods of 2 years until it is verified that the state of criminal dangerousness that gave rise to it has ceased.
4. The internment may not exceed the maximum limit of the sentence corresponding to the type of crime committed by the person not criminally liable.

Artigo 95º Cessation of the measure

1. The measure ceases when the state of criminal dangerousness that originated it ends or, if it continues, when the maximum limit of duration of the measure is reached, except in the circumstances referred to in paragraphs 3 and 4 of the preceding article.
2. The internment measure is mandatorily subject to review every 12 months.
3. If the existence of justifying cause for the cessation of internment is invoked, the court assesses the matter at any time.

Artigo 96º

Substitution of the internment measure

1. The internment measure may be substituted by release on probation or by expulsion from the national territory when applied to foreigners.
2. In the case of expulsion from the national territory, the provisions of Article 87 are correspondingly applicable.

Article 97 Freedom on probation

1. If from the review referred to in Article 95 it results that there are reasons to expect that the purpose of the measure can be achieved in an open environment, the court places the internee on freedom on probation.
2. The period of freedom on probation is fixed between a minimum of 2 years and a maximum of 5 years, and may not exceed the maximum limit fixed in paragraph 4 of Article 94.
3. The decision of freedom on probation may impose on the internee rules of conduct, necessary for the prevention of dangerousness, in terms corresponding to those referred to in Article 70, as well as the duty to submit to appropriate outpatient treatments and cure regimes and to undergo examinations and observations in the places indicated to him.

4. The agent whose execution of internment has been suspended is placed under tutelary supervision of social reintegration services.
5. If there are no grounds leading to the revocation of freedom on probation, upon expiry of its duration, the internment measure is declared extinct.
6. If, upon expiry of the period of freedom on probation, proceedings or incidents are pending that may lead to its revocation, the measure is declared extinct when the proceedings or incidents end and there is no ground for revocation.

Article 98

Revocation of freedom on probation

1. Freedom on probation is revoked when:
a) The behaviour of the non-imputable person reveals that internment is indispensable; or
b) The non-imputable person is sentenced to a custodial sentence and the conditions for suspension of execution are not met, under the terms of paragraph 1 of Article 68.
2. Revocation determines re-internment, with the provisions of Articles 94 and 95 being correspondingly applicable.

Article 99

Suspension of execution of internment

1. The court may order the suspension of internment if it is reasonable to expect that with the suspension the purpose of the security measure will be achieved and the suspension proves compatible with the defence of public order and social peace.
2. To the suspension of internment, the provisions of paragraphs 3 and 4 of Article 97 are correspondingly applicable.
3. The suspension of execution of internment may not be decreed if the agent is simultaneously sentenced to a custodial sentence and the conditions for suspension of its execution are not met, under the terms of paragraph 1 of Article 68.

4. The duration and cessation of suspension of internment are determined under the terms of Articles 94 and 95, respectively.
5. To the revocation of the decision to suspend the internment measure, the provisions of Article 98 apply.

SECTION II

OTHER SECURITY MEASURES

Article 100

Measure of professional disqualification

1. When a non-imputable person by reason of mental abnormality commits an act provided for in a legal type of crime related to the professional activity he exercises and there is well-founded fear that, while maintaining that occupation, he will continue to commit identical acts, the court may prohibit him from exercising the respective activity for a period of 1 to 5 years, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the personality of the non-imputable person.
2. The running of the period of disqualification is suspended during the period in which the agent is deprived of liberty by virtue of a procedural coercive measure, penalty or security measure.

Article 101

Prohibition from driving and revocation of licence for use and carrying of weapons

1. The non-imputable person who commits any of the acts provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 88 may be subject to disqualification from driving motor vehicles for a period of 2 to 6 years, whenever his personality generates well-founded fear of committing new acts of the same kind.
2. If the act committed by the non-imputable person corresponds to a crime related to the use of a weapon, the court may decree the revocation of the licence for use and carrying of weapons for a period of 5 to 10 years, whenever his personality generates well-founded fear of committing new acts of the same kind.

3. The provisions of paragraph 3 of Article 86 are correspondingly applicable.

CHAPTER IX

OTHER CONSEQUENCES OF CRIME

Article 102

Forfeiture of objects of crime

1. Objects that served or were intended to serve for the commission of a crime, or that were produced by it, are declared forfeited in favour of the State when by their nature or by the circumstances of the case they endanger the safety of persons or public order, or offer serious risks of being used for the commission of new crimes.
