rrr) Construct a normative system protecting property based on the legal types of crime most common in various criminal legislations such as theft, robbery, breach of trust and damage, structuring these types into simple and aggravated, considering the circumstances of value combined with the nature of the thing appropriated, the means and forms of action, violence, threats or coercion or any others capable of significantly increasing culpability or unlawfulness;
sss) Distinguish, still with regard to property, the norms typifying theft of use of vehicles, cases of violence after subtraction, usurpation of immovable property and alteration of landmarks, maintaining the incrimination of involuntary damage and defining the types of prohibited burning and fire as types of crime specifically of special damage by reason of the means and of danger in view of the consequences normally associated therewith;
ttt) Protect patrimony in general not only with the most usual incriminations in this matter such as fraud, extortion, receiving and bankruptcy or insolvency but, above all, bearing in mind the needs arising from new technologies in commerce and the responsibilities to be required of those who, managing goods or assets of others, do not conduct themselves adequately in that profession of administration, we refer to the crimes of computer fraud and the forms of harmful and abusive administration;

uuu) In modern and democratic societies, justice and its exercise are interests justifying the intervention of criminal protection, and punishment should be enshrined for falsity of procedural acts, forms of obstruction of jurisdictional activity, its non-exercise as denial of justice and violation of the respective secrecy;
vvv) Include incriminations relating to acts of bribery, prevarication by magistrate or official and by lawyer or public defender, as well as other activities of personal favouritism in the domain of justice, without forgetting the classic incriminations of false denunciation, simulation of crime and non-reporting;
www) In addition to the references contained in the last two numbers, conduct should be criminalised that in the exercise of public functions translates into situations of corruption, embezzlement, abuse of power or public force or economic participation in business by those exercising public offices or functions;
xxx) In an autonomous title, prevent through adequate incriminating norms, conduct of falsification of documents, technical notations, currency and sealed or stamped values, weights and measures, marks, stamps and seals, diversifying the respective punishments according to the nature, probative or fiduciary value and public use or destination of the falsified objects and provide for the possibility of seizure and loss of objects intended for the commission of these crimes;
yyy) Still in the same domain of the economy, incrimination should be made of acts that constitute tax fraud or situations of smuggling and customs evasion with regard to customs or border matters, in addition to maintaining criminal punishment of disobedience to the requisition of goods ordered by the government and conduct capable of disturbing, harming or preventing the performance of certain public acts such as public tender or judicial auction.

Article 3

Coordination with the Code of Criminal Procedure

The entry into force of the Penal Code referred to in this legislative authorisation law must be coordinated with the Code of Criminal Procedure and other regulatory legislation on criminal matters that is in force, as required by the legal certainty and coherence of the respective Legal Order.

Article 4 Duration and scope

The legislative authorisation granted by this law has a duration of 180 days, counted from the date of its entry into force.

Article 5 Entry into force

This legal instrument enters into force on the day immediately following its publication.

Approved on 23 September 2008

The President of the National Parliament in office, Maria da Paixão de Jesus da Costa

Promulgated on 7 October 2008. Let it be published.

The President of the Republic, Dr. José Ramos Horta

Law 6/2009, of 15 July
(First amendment to the Penal Code approved by Decree-Law No. 19/2009, of 8 April)

Life, from the moment of conception of the human being, must be protected. Taking this principle as established, it cannot be ignored that there are situations that may justify the termination of pregnancy. However, the gravity of the goods in conflict here, that is, the good (life of the pregnant woman) and the good (life of the foetus or embryo), both with the same value, only legitimises termination in extreme situations: when the sacrifice of one life is the only and last resort to save another and there is no way to maintain both.
It is therefore important to establish that the termination of pregnancy, the final word on which must always rest with the mother, who will decide in conscience, should only be permitted when it is the only means to prevent the death of the pregnant woman.
Thus, the National Parliament decrees, pursuant to article 92, paragraph 1 of article 95 and article 98 of the Constitution of the Republic, to have force of law, the following:

Article 1 Amendment to the Penal Code

Article 141 of the Penal Code approved by article 1 of Decree-law No. 19/2009, of 8 April, annexed thereto, shall have the following wording:

"Article 141 (...)

1.	(...)
2.	(...)
3.	(...)
4.	The provisions of the preceding paragraphs do not apply when the termination of pregnancy constitutes, in accordance with the state of medical knowledge and experience, and after

all possible actions have been carried out to save the life of the pregnant woman and that of the foetus or embryo, the only means to remove danger of death to the pregnant woman, provided it is performed, upon medical certification, by a doctor or other health professional under his or her direction in a public or officially recognised health establishment, and with the consent of the pregnant woman.
5.	The verification of the circumstances referred to in the preceding paragraph is certified in a medical certificate written and signed by a panel of three doctors, on a date prior to the termination, of which the doctor who performs or supervises the performance of the termination of pregnancy is not a member.
6.	Consent is given in writing in a document signed by the pregnant woman – after hearing, if possible, the spouse or the person living with her in conditions analogous to those of spouses – or by another person on her behalf, whenever possible with two days' notice in relation to the date of termination.
7.	In the case where the pregnant woman is a minor, consent is given by the legal representative.
8.	In the case where the pregnant woman of full age or emancipated is or becomes psychically incapable, consent is given, respectively and successively, by the spouse or the person living with her in conditions analogous to those of spouses, by the legal representative, by an ascendant or descendant, or, in their absence, by any relatives in the collateral line.
9.	When it is not possible to obtain the medical certificate referred to in paragraph 5, and/or it is not possible to obtain consent under the terms of paragraphs 6 to 8, and the performance of the termination is urgent and cannot be postponed, the doctor decides in conscience in view of the situation, resorting, whenever possible, to the opinion of other doctors.
10.	The panel referred to in paragraph 5 is constituted by doctors possessing adequate knowledge for the assessment of the circumstances that justify the termination of pregnancy, of which, whenever possible, a gynaecologist/obstetrician is a member.
11.	The doctors who issue the medical certificate and the doctors who perform or supervise the performance of the

termination of pregnancy must provide all necessary clarifications to the pregnant woman and, if applicable, to the persons referred to in paragraphs 7 and 8, namely the method of termination to be used, the effects of the termination, the possible consequences for the physical and psychological health of the pregnant woman.
12.	Doctors and other health professionals are recognised as having the right to conscientious objection with respect to any acts relating to the termination of pregnancy.
13.	Doctors and other health professionals who invoke conscientious objection must guarantee immediate intervention in the necessary acts, as well as the immediate care of the pregnant woman, by another health professional.
14.	Conscientious objection is expressed and communicated, through a written and signed document, to the clinical head of the health establishment where the objector provides service.

Law 13/2008, on 13 October (Legislative authorisation in criminal matters)

Law 6/2009, on 15 July (Amends article 41 of the Penal Code)

Decree-Law 19/2009, on 8 April (Approves the Penal Code)

To establish a legal system, those politically responsible included the elaboration and approval of Timor-Leste's Penal Code in their legislative priorities, in order to guarantee the fundamental rights and freedoms that the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste enshrines. This legal instrument comes from work that a commission, with Timorese and international technicians, developed following governmental guidance and following the limits and content that are established in the law concerning legislative authorisation that the National Parliament has already approved. The normative solution that is adopted here respects the social and cultural reality of the Timorese community and also accepts suggestions that came from national and international organisations, contributions that came from various judicial operators who work in Timor-Leste, and also teachings that are drawn from comparative law. We must look at this Code that is now approved, not as a final point, but as a fundamental stage to establish the Timorese legal order, open to future improvements that, in the future, the evolution of international law, judicial practice and the teaching of law advise. Thus,

Following the legislative authorisation received from Law 13/2009, on 13 October, its article 1, no. 2, and following article 96 of the Constitution, the Government decrees, to have the force of law:

Article 1
Approval of the Penal Code

The Penal Code, which is attached and forms part of this Decree-Law, is now approved. Article 2 Repealing norm

1. The Indonesian Penal Code that came into being in the legal order under article 1 of Law 10/2003 is repealed. 2. Legal provisions that are written in scattered legislation are repealed when
a) They foresee and punish facts criminalised in the Penal Code that is now approved; b) They enshrine solutions contrary to solutions adopted in the general part of this Penal Code. Article 3 Entry into force

This diploma and this Penal Code begin to be in force on the day following their publication. Approved in the Council of Ministers on 18 March 2009 Prime Minister
(Kay Rala Xanana Gusmão)

Minister of Justice (Lúcia M. B. F. Lobato)

Promulgated on 30/03/2009 Publish it. President of the Republic (José Ramos Horta)

Annex

PENAL CODE

I. The restoration of the independence of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste and the approval of its Constitution, in 2002, created the need for this country to adopt its own, modern legal order, which enshrines the fundamental rights that are included in constitutional precepts and reflects the country's social reality. We left the Indonesian Penal Code in force because a State must have its own criminal law, but that code is not adequate to the country's new reality and, often, presents legal solutions contrary to constitutional principles that have now come into being. Because the Timorese people have their own specificity and identity, their Penal Code must follow their own philosophy together with the principles and values that exist in modern societies, in order to respond to the demands that this country faces. A commission, with Timorese and international technicians, that Constitutional Government I established, elaborated a preliminary draft for a Penal Code; but, although the Law for Legislative Authorisation concerning criminal law was already approved, that preliminary draft did not receive promulgation until the legislature ended. When 2008 began, with another executive, a new bill for legislative authorisation to approve the Penal Code entered the National Parliament, work to revise this preliminary draft of the Penal Code proceeded again, and this diploma was amended and entered public discussion. Having received legislative authorisation, the Council of Ministers of Constitutional Government IV was able to approve this Penal Code. II. Book I of the Penal Code constitutes its general part, which gathers the criminal principles that are enshrined in the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste and in the conventions, treaties and international agreements that the Timorese legal order accepts. Because this code is based on the rule of law and democracy, its general part enshrines principles concerning human dignity, respect for each citizen's freedom and the State's responsibility to intervene only in situations where there is unsustainable injury to legal interests fundamental to life in society, with its right to punish the agent and its social obligation to reintegrate that agent into society. In a State under the rule of law there must also exist the principle of legality, the principle of fault and the principle of humanity. The enshrinement of the principle of legality as one of the fundamental principles of Criminal Law, provided for in article 31 of the Constitution, determines that an action or omission can be considered as a crime and, as a crime, give rise to punishment only in situations that the law foresees. To follow this principle, analogy cannot be applied to qualify any conduct as a crime and the tribunal cannot use analogical interpretation to interpret norms of the Penal Code to qualify any fact as a crime, define a state of dangerousness or determine a penalty or security measure. The principle of non-retroactivity in criminal law, which prevents retroactive application of criminal law, except when it favours the accused, is also a corollary of the principle of legality. The principle of humanity, enshrined in articles 29 and 32 of the Constitution, shows the way not to apply the death penalty and also penalties or security measures with a perpetual character or with unlimited or indefinite duration. The principle of necessity, the principle of proportionality and the principle of adequacy also show the way for the normative options that this Code follows and serve as the basis to apply each penalty and security measure, and each penalty and security measure shall be applied to protect legal interests essential to life in society and reinsert the agent into society. The principle of fault, as a presupposition to apply penalty, determines that there can be no penalty without fault (nulla poena sine culpa), and becomes a limitation on the State's power, because, thus, the measure of penalty cannot be heavier than the measure of fault. The principle of fault also shows the way for the regime of error concerning unlawfulness, the way to enshrine non-imputability due to age and due to psychic anomaly, which removes the agent's criminal liability because they lack fault. Concerning the legal consequences of punishable fact, we see that penalty must be executed with a pedagogical and resocialising perspective, and this code has many non-institutional means to apply sanctioning measures. When there is an alternative to apply a penalty privative or non-privative of liberty, the tribunal must give reasoned preference to the penalty that does not remove liberty when that penalty adequately and sufficiently achieves the purpose of punishment and satisfies the need to make reprobation and prevention of crime (article 62).

Thus, preference shall be given to applying alternative penalties, especially in situations concerning petty and medium criminality, with strong relevance for fine penalty and community service penalty, which are enshrined as the best means to ensure the delinquent's social reinsertion. Fine penalty shall be fixed by day, so that, thus, it can be adapted to the agent's fault and economic condition, with variation in the value that is fixed for each day of fine according to the convict's economic and financial situation and their personal charges. To differentiate fine penalty as principal penalty from fine that substitutes imprisonment penalty, specific rules are also established here to regulate the conversion of fine to imprisonment in situations where the agent does not pay the fine. Concerning community service, as a non-institutional sanctioning measure, this Code wants only to clarify and systematise some fundamental aspects in its regime and leave its development and application in each case to autonomous law. Imprisonment penalty must be applied only when it is seen that other penalties are not adequate to achieve the objectives of prevention and reprobation. The minimum duration of imprisonment penalty is established here at 30 days and its maximum duration at 25 years, with the possibility to raise that maximum limit to 30 years in cases that the law specially foresees. As a consequence that comes from the delinquent's social reinsertion, suspension of execution of imprisonment penalty can be applied in cases where the measure of concrete penalty does not exceed 3 years and the need to make prevention in the future does not close the way to that suspension. Suspension of execution of imprisonment penalty can also be applied with the condition that the convict fulfil obligations or rules of certain conduct or with the obligation to enter service for reinsertion supervision. This Code establishes for those non-imputable due to psychic anomaly security measures, with limited temporal duration, namely, internment measure, when their dangerousness justifies it. Measures with perpetual character cannot be applied, and that measure must stop when the dangerousness that justifies that application has ended, and, for foreigners, expulsion from the country can be applied in place of that security measure. In defence of the values and legal interests that are fundamental to living in society, this Code distinguishes crimes that have a public nature, which the State must protect directly, from those that, because they are not so serious, the person who has the right to present a complaint must first exercise that right, as the criminal procedural law establishes. Thus crimes shall be considered as semi-public that, in the special part of the Penal Code, according to the description in their legal types, create the obligation to exercise complaint. Concerning the extinction of criminal liability and the effects of that extinction, the general part of this Code fixes time limits for the prescription of criminal proceedings, penalties, security measures and accessory penalties and also defines situations that cause suspension of that time limit. But it establishes imprescriptibility for criminal proceedings and penalty concerning war crimes, crimes against peace and crimes against humanity and freedom. Finally, it also regulates other situations that cause extinction of criminal liability, such as the agent's death, amnesty and pardon. III. We know that the special parts of Penal Codes are what create the greatest impact on public opinion, because it is in that part that the goods, interests and values that in each society and in each historical moment can receive the protection of criminal law and, because of that, can enter the category of criminal-legal interests are chosen. In this Penal Code of Timor-Leste the legislator included in the normative solution that they adopted options that the Constitution has already enshrined as the sentiment of Timorese society. The systematisation that is adopted in this part comes from this country's history and reflects the interests and values that established this young nation. That is why title I of this book protects, respectively, peace, humanity and freedom as primordial values of democratic societies, with the respect that must be given as values that the fundamental law holds in its hierarchy and also following international obligations that this Country has already assumed when it subscribed to and ratified the Statute of the International Criminal Court. Title II enshrines crimes against persons and protects especially legal interests that are eminently personal, such as physical integrity, personal freedom, sexual freedom and protection of private life. Article 145 of this Code enshrines voluntary interruption of pregnancy as a crime. In this chapter and concerning crimes against physical integrity, there is special relevance for the crimes of ill-treatment of minor and ill-treatment of spouse, fundamental typification to affirm the rule of law and protect human rights in Timorese society. Also emphasised here is the criminalisation of enslavement and trafficking in persons, which comes from the humanist conception that showed the way for the elaboration of this code. Title III gathers crimes against life in democracy, with emphasis on crimes against public tranquillity, State security and life in society, and also electoral crimes and against public authority. In this title and in crimes against life in society there is special emphasis on the provision for specific typical crimes against the environment with punishment for conduct that causes unsustainable harm to fauna, flora and natural habitat; this shows that society's concern about the preservation of natural resources and protection of the environment has now grown. Title IV of this book enshrines protection of patrimony. Here the normative system gathers the legal types of crimes that are common in many criminal legislations, such as theft, robbery, abuse of trust, damage. These types are defined as simple or aggravated, as circumstances such as the value and nature of the appropriated thing, the means and form of action, violence, or other circumstances that can increase the agent's fault or unlawfulness. Titles V and VI gather crimes against the administration of justice and those that the agent commits when already holding public function; here punishment is enshrined for falsehood in procedural acts, various obstructions of jurisdictional activity, refusal to carry out that activity as denial of justice, and also shows, together with others, acts that constitute bribery and prevarication of magistrate, official, lawyer or public defender. It also criminalises activity concerning personal favouritism in justice and classic incriminations such as slanderous denunciation, simulation of crime and omission of criminal participation. Concerning the exercise of public function, in general, conduct of officials that constitutes crimes of corruption, embezzlement, abuse of power or abuse of public force, or economic participation in business by persons who exercise public office or function is also criminalised. In this matter the Penal Code broadens the concept of public official to include in that concept other analogous situations such as officials of international organisations, foreign public officials who exercise activity in Timor-Leste or persons who receive appointment to perform, alone or with others, activity that falls within the scope of public administrative or jurisdictional function. In title VII crimes of falsifying documents, technical notation, currency and sealed or stamped value, weight and measure, mark, die and stamp are typified, with various punishments, according to the nature, probative or fiduciary value and public use or destination of each falsified object, with provision concerning seizure and loss of objects that are to be used to commit these crimes. Finally, in title VIII crimes against the economy are typified, with punishment for money laundering, as the doctrine that has recently emerged concerning criminalisation of anti-economic activity, tax fraud and smuggling and embezzlement in the customs or border area. criminal punishment is maintained for disobedience to requisition that the Government makes for goods, and also conduct that can disturb, harm or impede any public act, such as the holding of public competition or judicial auction. It is also emphasised that, within this diploma, a way is sought to achieve balance for each abstract criminal framework, according to the type and gravity of each crime, hierarchy in the legal interests that each norm protects and the maximum limit enshrined for imprisonment penalty.In the legislative options that arise within this Penal Code, different treatment is given to the more serious criminality, to those for which, as a general rule, the legal type of crime provides only for imprisonment. In minor and medium criminality, as a general rule, the provision of the norm gives the court the alternative to, according to each case, use a custodial sentence or a fine, with the criminal policy orientation to recognize the fine as an autonomous penalty, not as a complementary penalty to the principal penalty. With the approval of this Code, the Timorese State has one more legal instrument, modern and adequate, to offer a better quality public-penal service and respect for the fundamental rights of citizens, magistrates, public defenders, lawyers, justice officials and also other judicial actors, who use these diplomas in their daily work, receive the obligation to undertake continuous training, the national legal order and the rule-of-law and democratic State will be strengthened further. BOOK I GENERAL PART TITLE I
APPLICATION OF PENAL LAW

SOLE CHAPTER GENERAL PRINCIPLES

Article 1 Principle of legality

1. An act or omission cannot be considered a crime before the law that already existed when that act or omission occurred defines that act or omission as a crime and establishes the respective penalty. 2. Security measures may only be applied to states of dangerousness whose presuppositions have already been defined by prior law. Article 2 Prohibition of analogy

Analogy may not be used to qualify an act or omission as a crime, define a state of dangerousness or determine their legal consequences. Article 3
Application of penal law in time

1. A person may not be punished for a fact that the law defines as a crime at the moment when he or she committed that fact when a subsequent law states that that fact is no longer a crime. 2. In that case, if there is already a conviction decision, even if final, the execution and penal effects of that conviction must cease. 3. The law that existed when the crime occurred applies to prior conduct when, in the concrete case, that law favours the agent and, if there is already a decision with res judicata, it is still possible to derive some advantage from that law. Article 4
Exceptional or temporary law

Exceptional or temporary law continues to apply to facts committed during the time when that law existed even though the time relating to its duration has already passed or the circumstances that determined that law have already ended. Article 5
Moment at which the fact is committed

It must be considered that the agent committed the crime at the moment when the act or omission occurred, regardless of the moment at which the typical result of that act or omission appears. Article 6

Place where the fact is committed
It must be considered that the agent committed the fact in the place where the act or omission occurred, in whole or in part only, and also in the place where the typical result of that fact appears or should properly appear. Article 7 Principle of territoriality

Save for other provisions in international treaties or conventions and regardless of the agent's nationality, Timorese penal law applies to facts committed in the territory of Timor-Leste and inside ships or aircraft with Timorese registration or flag. Article 8
Facts committed in non-national territory

Save for other provisions in international treaties or conventions, Timorese penal law applies to facts committed in places that are not Timorese territory in the following situations:
a) When that fact constitutes a crime provided for in articles 196 to 206, 229 to 242 and 307 to 313; b) When that fact constitutes a crime provided for in articles 123 to 135, 165 to 169 and 175 to 178, provided the agent is found in Timor-Leste and he or she cannot be extradited or there is a decision stating that he or she cannot be surrendered; c) When the agent committed that fact against a Timorese, provided that agent habitually resides in Timor-Leste and he or she is found in Timor-Leste; d) When a Timorese committed that fact or that fact was committed by a foreigner against a Timorese, provided the agent can be found in Timor-Leste, the law in the place where that fact occurred punishes that fact and that fact constitutes a crime that admits

extradition and in that case there cannot be authorization for that extradition; e) When that fact constitutes a crime for which the Timorese State, according to an international convention or treaty, has an obligation to try. Article 9
Limitations on the application of Timorese law

1. Timorese penal law may only be applied to facts committed in non-national territory when the agent has not yet received a final judgment in the place where he or she committed that fact or, if he or she has already received one, he or she has appealed and has not served the sanction, in whole or in part only. 2. Nevertheless, as the previous number, Timorese penal law will still apply, the fact will still be tried according to the law of the country in which the agent committed the fact, when that law favours the agent in that case. 3. In the case provided for in the previous number, the applicable sanction will be converted to its correspondent in the Timorese penal system or, if there is no correspondence, to the one that Timorese penal law provides for that fact. 4. When the agent must receive judgment in Timor-Leste and he or she has already received judgment in the place where he or she committed the fact, the penalty that he or she has already served abroad must also be taken into account. 5. The regime provided for in no. 2 does not apply to the crimes identified in the previous article, paragraphs a) and b). Article 10 Subsidiary application

Save for other provisions, the norms of this Code also apply to facts that special law punishes. TITLE II CRIME

CHAPTER I GENERAL PRESUPPOSITIONS
Article 11

Act and omission
1. When a result also enters into the legal type of a crime, the criminal fact embraces the adequate act to produce that result and also the omission of the adequate act to avoid that result, save when the law has another intention. 2. The omission that produces some result will only be punished when the omitter himself or herself has a legal obligation to avoid that result. 3. In the case written in the previous number, that penalty may receive extraordinary mitigation. Article 12 Criminal responsibility

1. Criminal responsibility for the infractions written in this Code falls only on singular persons and cannot be transmitted to other persons. 2. Collective persons may only receive criminal responsibility for infractions provided for in this Code or in special law when and under the conditions that the law expressly consecrates. Article 13
Responsibility for acting in another's name

A person who acts as a holder in the organ of a collective person or de facto association or as another person's representative will be punished, even though he or she does not have and the person he or she represents does have the condition, quality or relationship that is provided for in the type of crime. Article 14 Subjective imputation

Only facts that the agent committed with intent or, in cases that the law specially provides for, with negligence may give rise to punishment.

Article 15 Types of intent

1. A person acts with intent when, knowing beforehand that a fact fulfills the type of a crime, he or she acts with the intention to realize that fact. 2. A person also acts with intent when he or she knows clearly that his or her conduct will produce as a necessary consequence that fact that fulfills the type of a crime. 3. When the agent knows that his or her conduct may produce that fact that fulfills the type of a crime, there is intent when the agent still acts and agrees with that conduct. Article 16 Types of negligence

1. A person acts with negligence when, due to not having the care that, according to the circumstances, he or she must use and he or she can use:
a) He or she knows that his or her conduct may produce that fact that fulfills the type of a crime but acts even though he or she does not agree with that conduct; or
b) He or she does not know that his or her conduct may realize that fact. 2. The types of negligence referred to in the previous number become gross negligence when some circumstances show that the agent acted with recklessness or temerity, due to not following the basic care that he or she must follow in that case. Article 17
Error regarding circumstances

1. Error regarding an element of fact or element of law that enters into the legal type of a crime and also error regarding the prohibition that, reasonably, the agent must

know in order to be able to take consciousness regarding unlawfulness excludes intent. 2. The regime provided for in the previous number also includes error regarding the existence of presuppositions to exclude unlawfulness or presuppositions to exclude guilt. 3. The provisions of the previous numbers do not prevent the punishability of negligent conduct when the law provides for it and its presuppositions also exist.

Article 18
Error regarding unlawfulness

1. Ignorance of the law does not exclude the unlawfulness of conduct that violates that law. 2. Error regarding the unlawfulness of the fact, when unavoidable, excludes guilt. 3. When error regarding unlawfulness is avoidable, the penalty may receive extraordinary mitigation. Article 19 Aggravation due to result

When the penalty applicable to a fact must be aggravated according to the result that that fact produces, that aggravation exists only when that result can be imputed to the agent, at least, due to his or her negligence. Article 20 Unimputability due to age

1. Persons who are not yet 16 years old have penal unimputability. 2. The provisions of this diploma will also apply to young people who are already 16 years old but are not yet 21 years old in situations that autonomous legislation does not regulate, regarding the application and execution of criminal sanctions. Article 21 Unimputability due to psychic anomaly

1. A person will be considered unimputable when, due to psychic anomaly, at the moment when he or she committed the fact he or she could not evaluate the unlawfulness of that fact or make a decision according to that evaluation. 2. A person may receive a declaration as unimputable when, due to psychic anomaly, at the moment when he or she committed the fact, his or her capacity to evaluate the unlawfulness of that fact or to make a decision according to that evaluation was sensibly diminished. 3. The agent's proven incapacity to receive influence from the penalty may constitute an indication of the situation provided for in the previous number. 4. The agent does not lose imputability when he or she himself or herself provoked the psychic anomaly in order to commit that fact. CHAPTER II FORMS OF CRIME

Article 22 Preparatory act

Preparatory acts may not receive punishment, save for other legal provisions. Article 23 Attempt

Attempt exists when the agent begins execution of the crime that he or she has already made the decision to do, and does, all or some only, of the acts that can objectively produce its result and that result does not appear due only to circumstances that have no relation to the agent's will. Article 24 Punishability of attempt

1. In intentional crimes that may give rise to imprisonment with a maximum limit higher than 3 years and in cases that the law determines, attempt may give rise to punishment. 2. Save for other provisions, attempt gives rise to the penalty that will be applied to the consummated crime, with extraordinary mitigation. Article 25 Non-punishable attempt

Attempt may not receive punishment when it can be seen clearly that the means that the agent used has no aptitude or the object that is essential for the consummation of the crime does not exist. Article 26 Voluntary desistance

The agent's attempt may not be punished when he or she voluntarily desists from continuing the execution of the crime, prevents the crime from being consummated or its result from appearing or makes every effort to prevent the consummation or that result. Article 27
In participation

When there is participation, the agent's attempt does not give rise to punishment when he or she voluntarily prevents the crime from being consummated or the result of that crime from appearing, or makes every effort to prevent the consummation or that result, even though the other participants still execute or already consummate that fact. Article 28 Subsequent repentance

In crimes in which there is no violence or serious threat against persons, the penalty will receive extraordinary mitigation or, according to the circumstances, the agent will receive exemption from the penalty, when the damage has already been repaired, the thing has already been restituted or the situation

has already been legalized before there is participation or the competent authority receives a denunciation or complaint. CHAPTER III AGENTS OF CRIME

Article 29 Agent

Participation in committing a crime may be as authorship, instigation or complicity and many persons may participate together in one fact only

Article 30 Authorship

1. A person who executes the fact himself or herself or uses another person to execute that fact will be considered an author. 2. A person who, according to a tacit or express agreement, takes direct part in the execution or acts together with another person, putting together his or her effort with theirs, to commit one crime only will be considered a co-author

Article 31 Instigation

A person who convinces, directly and intentionally, another person to commit a crime will be punished as an author, provided that crime is already executed or its execution has already begun. Article 32 Complicity

1. A person who, intentionally, gives material or moral help to another person to commit some crime will be punished as an accomplice. 2. The penalty that is applied to the type of unlawful act will be applied to the accomplice, with extraordinary mitigation. Article 33

Guilt in participation
Each participant will be punished according to his or her guilt, regardless of the punishment of the other participants or the extent to which the other participants have guilt. Article 34 Unlawfulness in participation

1. When the unlawfulness of the fact or the degree of that unlawfulness depends on some special quality or relationship of the agent, it is enough that a co-participant has that quality or relationship in order to give rise to applying the respective penalty to all co-participants, save when the incriminating norm has another intention. 2. When, due to the rule that the previous number provides for, a more serious penalty must be applied to some co-participant, considering the circumstances that characterize that case, another penalty may be put in the place of that penalty which, if that rule did not exist, would be applied to him or her. CHAPTER IV CONCURRENCE AND CONTINUED CRIME

Article 35 Crimes in concurrence

1. To know how many crimes the agent committed, it is necessary to count how many types of crime he or she has already committed or how many times his or her conduct fulfills the type of one crime only. 2. For the provisions of the following article, the situation in which the agent, having committed one crime, commits another crime again before receiving a conviction with a final decision in judgment will be considered as concurrence. Article 36 Punishment in concurrence

1. When several crimes enter into concurrence, only one penalty must be applied, whose minimum limit is the highest concrete penalty among all the concrete penalties applied to each crime and whose maximum limit is the material sum of all the partial penalties
2. In concurrence, the maximum limit of the framework may not exceed 600 days for the fine penalty or 30 years for the imprisonment penalty, even though in situations where the material sum of the partial penalties exceeds that legal limit. 3. To determine the single penalty, the court must consider, together, the facts and the agent's personality. Article 37 Sanctions in concurrence

1. When some applied penalties are fines and some are imprisonment, the nature of each penalty will still remain as such. 2. Accessory penalties and security measures, even though only one of the applicable laws or only one of the previous decisions provides for them, will still be maintained. Article 38
Suspended penalty in legal cumulation

1. Imprisonment with suspended execution may only enter into cumulation with other imprisonment penalties when:
a) All those penalties are imprisonment with suspended execution and that cumulation does not prevent the execution of the single penalty from still being suspended; b) If that cumulation is done with effective imprisonment, there are circumstances that give rise to having already revoked the suspension of the execution of that imprisonment, even though that cumulation does not exist; c) The periods for suspension of the suspended penalties to be cumulated are not the same, or, if those periods are the same, the compliance of each enters into different phases, and the court establishes only one period for suspension, according to the necessity of prevention and the circumstances that characterize that case

Article 39
Concurrence with supervening knowledge

When a decision has already obtained res judicata but, before that penalty receives compliance, prescription or extinction, there is knowledge that the agent enters into some situation that the previous articles describe, the rules that are written in them will be applied. Article 40
Crime with other infractions

When one fact constitutes a crime and a contra-ordenação at the same time, the agent will be punished for the crime, but the accessory sanction provided for the other infractions will also be applied. Article 41 Continued crime

1. A person who does several times the same type of crime or does different types of crime that fundamentally protect only one legal good, with essentially homogeneous conduct and in only one external situation that considerably diminishes his or her guilt, commits only one continued crime,
save for crimes that protect eminently personal goods. 2. The penalty that will be applied to the conduct that is more serious than the others that enter into that continuation will be applied to the continued crime. Article 42
Several norms in concurrence

For facts that several legal provisions qualify, in whole or in part only, as a crime and do not enter into the situations that the previous articles describe, only one incriminating norm will be applied according to the following rules:
a) The special norm will be applied with prejudice to the general norm;

b) Preference will be given to the subsidiary norm over the principal norm; c) The norm that is broader and more complex consumes the norm that provides for facts subsumable in its provision. CHAPTER V CAUSES OF EXCLUSION

SECTION I
CAUSES THAT EXCLUDE UNLAWFULNESS

Article 43 Exclusion of unlawfulness

1. A fact may not give rise to punishment as a crime when the legal order considered as a totality has removed its unlawfulness. 2. Namely, the fact that a person committed when exercising his or her right, in legitimate defense, in a state of justifying necessity or with consent regarding a freely available legal interest is not unlawful. Article 44 Legitimate defense

The agent's action necessary to prevent an illegitimate, imminent or already executing but not yet finished aggression against the legal interests that the legal order protects of the agent or another person will be considered as legitimate defense. Article 45
State of justifying necessity

The fact that the agent committed as a means to prevent a current danger that threatens the legally protected interests of that agent or another person is not unlawful, when the following requirements exist:

a) The interest to be protected has sensible superiority when it is compared with the sacrificed interest; and
b) It is reasonable to sacrifice the injured party's interest when one sees the nature or value of the interest that is facing danger beforehand. Article 46 Conflict of duties

1. The agent's conduct is not unlawful when, when there is a conflict to comply with several legal duties or legitimate orders of authority, he or she complies with the duty or order with a value equal to or greater than the duty or order that he or she sacrifices. 2. The duty of hierarchical obedience ends when it gives rise to a crime. Article 47 Consent

1. Together with the cases that the law specially provides for, consent excludes the unlawfulness of the fact when it regards a freely available legal interest and that fact does not offend good customs. 2. Consent may be shown by any means that expresses the serious, free and enlightened will of the holder of the legally protected interest, and may be revoked according to that holder's wish until the execution of the fact. 3. Consent has efficacy only when it comes from a person who is older than 16 years and has the necessary discernment to assess the meaning and scope of that consent at the moment when he or she gives that consent. 4. When the agent does not know that that consent exists, he or she will be punished with the penalty that applies to attempt.

5. Presumed consent will be equated to effective consent, which exists when the situation in which the agent acts gives rise to thinking reasonably that, if the holder of the legally protected interest knew the circumstances in which the agent committed that fact, he or she would already give consent effectively to that fact. SECTION II
CAUSES THAT EXCLUDE GUILT

Article 48
Excess in legitimate defense

1. Means that, due to their species and degree in their utilization, become excessive for the agent's action to defend himself or herself may give rise to extraordinary mitigation in the penalty that will be applied to the crime. 2. Excess in the means used in legitimate defense due to non-censurable perturbation, fear or panic excludes the agent's guilt. Article 49
State of exculpating necessity

1. A person who committed an unlawful fact adequate to prevent a current danger that threatens the life, physical integrity, honor or liberty of the agent or another person acts without guilt, when there is no other way to remove that danger and, according to the circumstances that characterize that case, it is not reasonable to request other conduct from the agent. 2. When the danger that threatens legal interests that the previous article does not fear and the other presuppositions that are written in it all appear, the penalty may receive extraordinary mitigation or, exceptionally, the agent may receive exemption from the penalty. Article 50 Exculpating undue obedience

An official who complies with an order acts without guilt when he or she does not know that that order makes him or her commit some crime and the circumstances that he or she sees beforehand do not show clearly that his or her conduct is a crime. TITLE III CIRCUMSTANCES

SOLE CHAPTER GENERAL RULES

Article 51 Measure of penalty

1. To determine the measure of the penalty, according to the limits that the law defines, one must see the agent's guilt and the necessity to do prevention. 2. To determine the concrete penalty, the court sees all the circumstances that do not enter into the type of crime and favour or disfavour the agent. 3. In the sentence, the grounds for the measure of the penalty will be expressly referred to. Article 52 General aggravating circumstances

1. Prior, contemporaneous or subsequent circumstances that do not enter into the legal type and show that the unlawfulness of the fact, the agent's conduct or the agent's guilt is stronger and increase the necessity of the penalty will be considered as general circumstances that aggravate the agent's responsibility. 2. The following may constitute general aggravating circumstances, together with others:
a) The agent executes the crime with disloyalty, which exists when the agent uses treason, ambush, waiting, disguise; b) The agent commits the crime against a person by means, mode or form that, directly or indirectly,

guarantees that he or she will not face danger that may come from the defense that the victim may present; c) The agent does the crime with fraud, deceit, excess of power or authority or takes advantage of circumstances regarding place, time to do that crime; d) The agent receives payment, value or some reward to commit that crime; e) The agent does that crime due to racist motivation or discriminatory sentiment regarding the victim's gender, ideology, religion, ethnicity, nationality, sex or sexual orientation, illness or physical diminishment; f) The agent has a special obligation not to commit that crime, to prevent that crime or to participate in giving punishment for that crime or uses the public authority that he or she has or says that he or she has to do that crime; g) In cases where there is no recidivism, the agent does one or more crimes with the same nature within the previous 3 years starting from the moment when he or she committed the crime for which he or she is facing the current judgment, independent of the moment when he or she received conviction; h) The agent does that crime on the occasion when he or she does another crime to more easily commit one or several crimes; i) The agent committed that crime with facility due to entering or attempting to enter the victim's residence, using poison, flood, fire, explosion, shipwreck or damage to a boat or weapon; j) The participation of two or more persons gives facility to commit that crime or take advantage of the consequences of that crime; k) The agent intentionally and inhumanly increases the victim's suffering, by producing suffering that is not necessary to consummate that crime or other acts regarding spoliation, cruelty or destruction that are not necessary to do the crime; l) The victim is the agent's spouse or person who lives with the agent as wife with husband at that time or before, or his or her ascendant, descendant, sibling, adoptee or adopter; m) The victim is a very vulnerable person due to his or her age, illness or physical or psychic deficiency, when that circumstance does not enter into the legal type. Article 53 Recidivism

1. A person who, having received a conviction with a final sentence in judgment to effective imprisonment higher than 6 months, he or she alone or together with another person, in any way, committed an intentional crime for which gives rise to effective imprisonment exceeding 6 months, will be punished as a recidivist, when, according to the circumstances that characterize that case, the agent must receive censure because the conviction or convictions that have already fallen on him or her were not enough to prevent him or her from doing another crime. 2. There is no recidivism when, from the moment when the agent committed one crime until the moment when he or she committed another crime and not counting for that period the time in which he or she is serving a procedural measure, penalty or security measure that removes liberty, 4 years have already passed. 3. When there is recidivism, the minimum limit of the applicable penalty will rise by one third, but its maximum limit does not change and that aggravation may not be higher than the highest penalty that has already been applied in the previous convictions. Article 54 Criminal habituality

1. When the agent committed an intentional crime that, concretely, gives rise to effective imprisonment higher than 1 year in the following circumstances cumulatively:
a) The agent has already committed before three or more intentional crimes that gave rise to effective imprisonment;

b) From each of those crimes until the following crime, three years have not yet passed; c) The evaluation of the facts and of the agent's personality together shows that the agent has a tendency to do crimes; the penalty regarding the crime committed will be applied with its minimum and maximum limits having risen by one third. 2. This provision prevails over the rule of punishment due to recidivism itself. Article 55 General mitigating circumstances

1. Circumstances that appear before the agent does the crime, appear together with the crime or have passed the crime and then appear and favour the agent will be considered general mitigating circumstances for the agent's responsibility. 2. The following circumstances may become general mitigating circumstances, together with others:
a) The causes of exclusion that are written in the previous chapter when the requirements provided for the causes of exclusion to produce some effect do not exist; b) The agent committed the fact because some fact produced violent emotion, obsession or passionate state with a nature similar to these or reacts immediately to provocation; c) The agent presents himself or herself to the authority before he or she knows that there is a criminal proceeding against him or her; d) The agent confesses the crime that he or she did himself or herself or gives a decisive contribution to knowing the circumstances that characterize that criminal action; e) There are acts that show that the agent already repents; f) The agent's intent or negligence is diminished; g) The victim and agent are already reconciled. Article 56
Circumstances of extraordinary mitigation

1. Together with the cases that the law expressly provides for, the penalty provided for in the legal type will be extraordinarily mitigated when there are circumstances that appear before the agent does the crime, appear together with the crime or have passed the crime and then appear and, together or alone only, markedly diminish the unlawfulness of the conduct, the agent's guilt or the necessity of the penalty. 2. For the effects of the previous number, the following circumstances will be considered, together with others:
a) The agent acted due to serious threat or due to receiving influence from a person on whom he or she depends or whom he or she must obey; b) The agent acted due to honorable motive, because the victim himself or herself requested or tempted him or her or due to unjust provocation or offense that he or she did not deserve; c) The agent repairs the prejudice that he or she produced or reduces the effects of that prejudice, at any moment but before the judgment begins; d) The crime occurred a long time ago and the agent has had good conduct continuously; e) The agent has sensibly diminished imputability. Article 57
Degree of extraordinary mitigation

1. When there is extraordinary mitigation of the penalty, the following rules will be applied to the limits of the applicable penalty:
a) The maximum limit of the imprisonment penalty will fall by one third; b) The minimum limit of the imprisonment penalty will fall by one fifth when it is equal to or higher than 3 years and falls to the legal minimum when it is shorter than 3 years; c) The maximum limit of the fine penalty will fall by one third and its minimum limit will fall to the legal minimum; d) When the maximum limit of the imprisonment penalty is not higher than 3 years, a fine penalty may be put in the place of the imprisonment penalty, within the general limits. 2. The concrete penalty that results from extraordinary mitigation may receive substitution, together with suspension, according to the general rules.

Article 58 Concurrence of circumstances

1. When some circumstance that modifies the abstract framework of the legal type appears, the regime of extraordinary mitigation that is written in the previous article will be applied. 2. When two or more circumstances that modify the abstract framework of the legal type appear together, only one will be considered according to the provisions of the previous article, the others will be considered as general mitigating circumstances to determine the measure of the penalty. TITLE IV
LEGAL CONSEQUENCES OF CRIME

CHAPTER I GENERAL PROVISIONS

Article 59
Penalties and security measures
1.The death penalty or penalties or security measures that deprive liberty with a perpetual character or unlimited or indefinite duration shall not be applied. 2. For a single act, a penalty together with a security measure that deprives liberty shall not be applied. Article 60
Limits of penalties and security measures

1. The concrete penalty shall not be more severe than the severity of the guilt. 2. Security measures based on the dangerousness of the agent who committed the act provided for as a crime shall last until it is seen that the criminal dangerousness that gave rise to that measure has ceased and its duration shall not be longer than the maximum limit of the penalty applicable to the said crime. Article 61
Purpose of penalties and security measures36

36 (61) Purpose (s) - objective

Penalties and security measures shall be applied to protect legal interests essential to life in society and to achieve the agent's reintegration into society. Article 62
Choice of penalty and security measure

1. When either a penalty depriving liberty or a penalty not depriving liberty may be applied to a crime, the court shall choose the latter when that penalty is better and sufficient to achieve the purpose of the penalty. 2. To choose a security measure to apply to an agent whose dangerousness has already been shown in the proceedings, the agent's personality and what treatment is best for that situation must be considered. Article 63
Effects of penalties and security measures

A penalty or security measure shall not, as a necessary effect, extinguish civil, professional or political rights. Article 64
Execution of penalties or measures depriving liberty

1. An agent who receives a sentence with effective imprisonment or is subject to an internment measure may obtain conditional release or release on probation. 2. Save for other provisions, conditional release must be granted to a convict who has already served two-fifths of the penalty. 3. The convict's consent must be obtained to grant conditional release to him or her, except in the situation provided for in the previous number. 4. Only the law shall establish the prerequisites and conditions for granting conditional release and also the rights and obligations of the released persons and the prerequisites and conditions for executing effective imprisonment in an open regime. Article 65

Concurrence of penalties and measures depriving liberty
1. When an agent receives a sentence of effective imprisonment and is subject to an internment measure, that measure must be executed first and discounted from the imprisonment. 2. If the internment measure ends, the court shall place the agent on conditional release when he or she has already served the time corresponding to part of the penalty and his or her release appears compatible37 with the defense of the legal order and social peace. CHAPTER II IMPRISONMENT

Article 66
Duration of imprisonment

1. Imprisonment must last at least 30 days and shall not exceed 25 years. 2. In cases that the law specifically provides for, imprisonment may last up to 30 years. 3. The maximum limit of the penalty referred to in the previous number shall not be exceeded. Article 67
Substitution of imprisonment with a fine

1. In place of an applied imprisonment not exceeding 1 year, a fine may be imposed for the same period, up to the maximum legal limit, when the need to prevent future crime does not require the serving of imprisonment and, according to the circumstances surrounding that case, the court sees that the execution should not be suspended. 2. When the convict does not pay the substitute fine or any installment thereof, he or she must still serve the imprisonment that was first fixed, but the convict may still pay the fine in full or provide property to guarantee

37 (65) - Compatible; this goes well with (something else); which does not prejudice (something else). Compatible with the defense of social peace = does not prejudice the defense of social peace

that payment to halt that serving, discounting the installments already paid. 3. The court must give reasons for its decision not to make the substitution when the applied penalty gives rise to that substitution. Article 68
Suspension of execution of imprisonment

1. The court may suspend the execution of an applied imprisonment not exceeding 3 years, for 1 year to 5 years, beginning from the transit in rem judicatam of the decision. 2. The reasons for the suspension must be included in the decision, namely, the agent's personality, the circumstances surrounding the commission of the crime, his or her previous conduct, conditions concerning his or her life and, especially, the predictability concerning his or her future conduct. 3. The court must give reasons for its decision not to suspend the execution when the applied penalty gives rise to that suspension. Article 69
Suspension of imprisonment with duties

1. To suspend the execution of imprisonment the court, as a condition, may order the convict to fulfill certain duties, not humiliating, to repair the harm of the crime. 2. The following duties may be conditions for the suspension:
a) The convict repairs or provides a guarantee to repair the damage caused by the crime, within a determined period; b) The convict presents an apology to the victim in public; c) The convict performs some task related to the crime committed; d) The convict delivers money to the State or to a charitable institution with significance for the convict's reinsertion. 3. An obligation that orders him or her to do something that cannot reasonably38 be asked of him or her to do shall not be applied to the convict. 4. Until the period for the suspension ends, the obligation being applied to the convict may be changed when some supervening relevant circumstance arises or has passed that the court only now learns of some relevant circumstance. 5. The provision of the previous article,
no. 2, also applies here. Article 70 Rules of conduct

1. The court may order the convict to comply, during the suspension period, with rules of conduct that facilitate his or her integration into society, namely:
a) Not exercising certain professions; b) Not frequenting certain environments or places; c) Not residing in certain places or regions; d) Not associating with certain persons, not letting certain persons stay in his or her home or not receiving certain persons; e) Not frequenting certain associations or participating in certain meetings; f) Not possessing objects that may facilitate his or her committing a crime; g) Presenting himself or herself to the court, social reinsertion technicians or non-police entities periodically. 2. Having obtained the convict's consent, the court may also order him or her to undergo medical treatment or cure in an adequate institution. 3. The provisions of the previous article,
nos. 3 to 5, also apply here. Article 71
Suspension of imprisonment with supervision

1. When simple or conditional suspension of imprisonment is not sufficient to guarantee the delinquent's recovery and to dissuade him or her from criminal activity, the court may apply the suspension together with supervision by the social reinsertion service for the convict during the suspension period, having obtained the convict's agreement, when possible. 2. The social reinsertion service together with the Public Prosecutor and the judge who gave the sentence shall, having heard the convict, elaborate a plan for social reinsertion, which, having obtained approval from the court, shall be fulfilled with the assistance of an official or the reinsertion service. 3. All obligations applied to the convict must be included in the readaptation plan; the court may order the convict to comply with the obligations and rules of conduct that are written in articles 69 and 70 or other obligations that are of interest to the readaptation plan, namely:
a) Obtaining treatment or internment in an adequate establishment, when circumstances require; b) Complying with the summons of the magistrate responsible for execution or social reinsertion technicians; c) Receiving visits from social reinsertion technicians and providing information to those technicians concerning his or her means of living; d) Informing the social reinsertion technicians about changes to his or her residence or employment, and also about absences from his or her residence when longer than 8 days; e) Obtaining prior authorization from the magistrate responsible for execution before going abroad. 4. The provision of article 68,
no. 2, also applies here. Article 72
Modification of the suspension regime

When during the time that the execution of imprisonment is suspended the convict does not fulfill the obligations being applied to him or her or receives judgment and sentence for another crime, the court, considering the circumstances in which, may alter the regime that was fixed for the suspension of the execution of imprisonment, modify the obligations or give a solemn warning to the convict, according to the circumstances of each case. Article 73 Revocation of suspension

1. When during the time that the execution of imprisonment is suspended the convict receives judgment and sentence for another crime or intentionally relapses into non-compliance with the rules or obligations being applied to him or her, and it is seen that modification of the suspension regime cannot be made or that modification is not sufficient, the court shall revoke the suspension. 2. The suspension of the execution of imprisonment must indeed be revoked when, during the respective period, the convict commits an intentional crime for which he or she receives a sentence of effective imprisonment. 3. The revocation of the suspension does not give the convict the right to receive back the installments that he or she has already made during the suspension and because of the suspension. Article 74
Extinction of imprisonment
When the suspension of the execution of imprisonment is not revoked, after the respective period has passed, that penalty and its effects shall be extinguished

CHAPTER III FINE PENALTY

Article 75
Duration of fine penalty

1. A fine penalty shall be fixed with its minimum at 10 days and its maximum at 360 days, save for other provisions. 2. One day of fine corresponds to an amount, which the court fixes according to the convict's economic and financial situation and his or her personal burdens, between one United States dollar to 200 United States dollars. 3. When the circumstances surrounding the case justify, the court may authorize payment of the fine within a period of 1 year, or allow payment by installments39, but the last installment must be paid within 2 years beginning from the day that the sentence transits in rem judicatam. Article 76
Alternative imprisonment for fine penalty

The decision that applies a fine penalty directly shall also fix alternative imprisonment, for the time that corresponds to that fine, reduced by two-thirds. Article 77
Reduction or exemption of fine penalty

1. When a convict with a fine penalty does not fulfill that penalty because of circumstances that arose after the sentence and make impossible or difficult the fulfillment of the fine, but are not imputable to him or her, the court may decree reduction or exemption from that penalty. 2. The provision of the previous number also applies to a fine penalty that substitutes imprisonment. CHAPTER IV
COMMUNITY SERVICE PENALTY

Article 78 Community service

1. A community service penalty is one in which the convict must perform unpaid service for a public body or other entity that the court considers to be in the public interest, namely having obtained the convict's consent. 2. The court fixes the time for the convict to perform that service and, for that, shall convert the imprisonment fixed in the sentence at one day per one hour of service, but shall not exceed 240 hours. 3. The work may be done within normal service hours or not, continuously or not, but on each day shall not be longer than the time established in the regime for extraordinary hours and shall not prejudice the survival40 of the convict or the convict's family. 4. Unjustified non-compliance in performing community service gives rise to serving the penalty that was first applied, discounting the days on which that work was already done, according to the provision of no. 2. Article 79 Requirements

1. The court may apply a community service penalty in place of imprisonment not longer than 1 year or a fine penalty when it sees that by that means the purpose of punishment can be realized adequately and sufficiently and, concerning imprisonment, reasons concerning criminal prevention show that it is better not to suspend the execution or substitute with a fine. 2. The convict's consent must be obtained to apply community service, which, concerning a substitute fine, may be decreed in the sentence or in a subsequent order, namely the convict presents a request before there is an order for seizure in the execution proceedings for not paying the fine. 3. The provision of article 67,
no. 3, shall apply here. Article 80
Suspension, reduction or exemption of penalty

When the convict does not fulfill the work, because of circumstances that arose after its application and make impossible or difficult that fulfillment and cannot be imputed to him or her, the regime provided for in article 77 shall apply. Article 81 Complementary legislation

40 (78) Survival (s) – minimum material condition needed to live. A special diploma shall regulate the other conditions for applying the community service penalty and the functioning of the service to execute the work. CHAPTER V ADMONISHMENT PENALTY

Article 82 Admonishment

When considering the agent guilty of committing a crime that gives rise to an abstract imprisonment not exceeding 3 years or a fine, the court may give only an admonishment to him or her, when, cumulatively:
a) The damage of the criminal conduct has already been repaired; b) The agent is a primary delinquent; c) The admonishment is sufficient to achieve criminal prevention and to recover the delinquent. Article 83
Execution of admonishment penalty

The court shall make the admonishment, with a solemn and adequate oral reprimand to the convict, at a public hearing, when the decision that applies that penalty has already transited in rem judicatam. CHAPTER VI ACCESSORY PENALTY

Article 84 General principle

1. The law may establish for certain crimes prohibition from exercising certain rights or professions. 2. An accessory penalty shall be cumulated with other accessory penalties, must be applied together with the principal penalty and its duration must be fixed according to the severity of the guilt. Article 85
Temporary suspension from exercising public function

1. When sentencing a person who exercises a public office with effective imprisonment the court must decree suspension of the exercise of that function during the period that he or she serves the penalty, when the convict does not receive disciplinary dismissal
2. The effects that, according to the respective law, accompany the disciplinary sanction of suspension from exercising the function shall be applied to the convict, during the suspension period. 3. The provisions of the previous numbers shall apply to professions or activities that require a public title, authorization or approval from a public authority to exercise. Article 86 Prohibition from exercising functions

1. A holder of public office, public official or agent of the Administration who, when exercising the activity for which he or she obtained election or appointment to hold, receives a sentence of imprisonment exceeding 3 years may receive prohibition from exercising those functions, for 2 years to 5 years, when the following circumstances arise:
a) The agent committed the act with flagrant and serious abuse of his or her function or with manifest and serious violation of the duty that he or she must fulfill in that function; b) The agent shows manifest unworthiness or incapacity to exercise that office; c) The nature of the act destroys the trust that must exist to exercise that function. 2. The provision of the previous number also applies to professions or activities that a person must have a public title, authorization or approval from a public authority to exercise. 3. The time in which the accused is deprived of his or her liberty because of a coercive measure, penalty or security measure does not count as the period of prohibition. 4. The accessory penalty provided for in this article shall not be applied when, because of those very acts, the security measure provided for in article 100 is already being applied. 5. When applying the provisions of this article, the court's sentence must be notified to the public authority on which the official depends. Article 87 Expulsion

1. A foreign citizen who receives a sentence for committing a crime that gives rise to imprisonment exceeding 3 years and has not yet resided in Timor-Leste for 15 years may receive expulsion from the national territory:
a) For a period up to 2 years, when he or she has already resided here for more than 10 years; b) For a period up to 5 years, when he or she has already resided here for more than 5 years but has not yet reached 10 years; c) For a period up to 10 years, when he or she has not yet resided here for 5 years. 2. The expulsion referred to in the previous number shall be applied when in the concrete case it is indeed necessary for internal security, public health or to prevent criminal activity from continuing. 3. The expulsion penalty shall be executed without regard to the total or partial serving of the principal penalty. Article 88 Prohibition from driving

1. Disqualification from driving a motor vehicle for 3 months to 2 years may be applied to a person who receives a sentence:
a) For a crime provided for in articles 207 to 209; b) For a crime committed with a motorized vehicle and executed with facility indeed because of using a motorized vehicle; c) For a crime of disobedience for refusing to take the test that the law establishes to determine whether a person is driving a motorized vehicle under the disturbing effect on his or her physical, mental
or psychological aptitude of alcohol, psychotropic substances or products with analogous effect. 2. The provision of article 86,
no. 3, shall also apply here. Article 89
Cancellation of license to possess weapons

1. When there is a sentence for an intentional crime committed with a weapon, considering the circumstances and the seriousness of the conduct, the court may decree cancellation41 of the license to use and possess weapons, for 2 years to 8 years. 2. The provision of article 86,
no. 3, shall also apply here. CHAPTER VII
DETERMINATION OF PENALTIES

Article 90 General principle

1. When the law fixes a penalty, that penalty refers to that crime in its consummated form. 2. To obtain the abstract penalty framework from which the concrete penalty shall be determined:
a) The modifying circumstances of recidivism and criminal habituality provided for in articles 53 and 54, respectively, shall be applied to the abstract penalty of the consummated crime; b) Following the provision of the previous paragraph or starting from the abstract penalty of the consummated crime, when there are modifying circumstances, the situation that gives rise to extraordinary mitigation shall be considered, when it exists. Article 91
Concrete determination of penalty

1. Having obtained the abstract penalty framework according to the previous article, the court shall assess all circumstances that do not enter into the legal type and do not enter into the valuation as provisions of the previous article but aggravate or diminish the convict's responsibility. 2. Based on the valuation of those latter circumstances, the court shall fix the exact measure of penalty that it considers necessary to protect legal interests essential to life in

41 (89) Cancellation (s) - Annulment

society and for the agent's social reintegration, within the framework that is fixed within the limits of the type or within the limits that result from the provisions of the previous article. 3. The severity of the penalty applied to the convict may never exceed the severity of the guilt. Article 92
Special case of fine penalty

To apply a fine penalty the provisions of this chapter shall be applied to determine the time concerning the duration of the fine, without prejudice to the provision of article 75,
no. 2, to calculate the amount that corresponds to the fine for each day. CHAPTER VIII SECURITY MEASURES

SECTION I INTERNMENT MEASURE

Article 93 Prerequisites

When a person not imputable according to the provisions of article 21 commits an act described in the legal type of a crime, the court may order his or her admission to an adequate establishment, when, because of his or her psychic anomaly and the nature and seriousness of the committed act, there is a basis to fear that he or she may commit another typical act corresponding to a crime against persons or a crime of common danger. Article 94 Duration

1. When the act that the imputable person committed gives rise to imprisonment up to 3 years, the internment shall not be longer than 1 year. 2. When the imputable person committed an act that corresponds to a crime against persons or a crime of common danger punishable with imprisonment of 5 years or more, the internment shall not be shorter than 3 years, except when it is seen that release does not prejudice the defense of public order and social peace. 3. When the imputable person committed a crime punishable with a penalty (imprisonment) exceeding 8 years and it is better not to release him or her because otherwise he or she may again commit a similar crime, the internment may be extended by a further period of 2 years, successively42, until it is seen that the state of dangerousness that gave rise to that prolongation has ceased. 4. The internment shall not be longer than the maximum limit of the penalty applicable to the crime that the accused committed. Article 95 Cessation of measure

1. The internment measure ends when the criminal dangerousness that gave rise to that measure ceases or, if that dangerousness continues, when the maximum duration of that measure has already been reached, except in the circumstances written in the previous article,
