A person who abusively uses a signal or call for alarm or help, or pretends to make people believe that they need another person's help due to disaster, danger or situation of collective need shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine. 2.The same penalty shall also be imposed on anyone who improperly or abusively uses a uniform, attire, costume or insignia that identifies a public or international activity, authority or institution as a means to facilitate the commission of some illicit act. Article 195 Usurpation of function

1. Anyone who, without authorization, exercises a function or practices an act that only a public official, military command or public security force may practice, thus presenting themselves, expressly or tacitly, as a person having such capacity, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Anyone who exercises a profession that, according to law, only persons having a title or fulfilling certain conditions may exercise, thus presenting themselves, expressly or tacitly, as a person having such title or fulfilling such conditions, shall also be punished with the same penalty. 3. Anyone who continues to exercise a public function despite having received official notification of their dismissal or suspension from such function shall also be punished with the same penalty. CHAPTER II
AGAINST STATE SECURITY

Article 196 Treason against the homeland

Anyone who, through violence, threat of violence, usurpation or abuse of sovereign function, prevents or attempts to prevent the exercise of national sovereignty in the territory of Timor-Leste or in any part of that territory or endangers the integrity of the national territory, as a means to submit or surrender to foreign sovereignty, shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 30 years. Article 197
Service or collaboration with enemy armed forces

1. A Timorese citizen who collaborates with a foreign country or group or with their representatives or serves under the flag of a foreign country in time of war or armed action against Timor-Leste shall be punished with imprisonment from 15 to 25 years. 2. Preparatory acts relating to the facts described in the previous number shall give rise to imprisonment from 3 to 15 years. 3. A Timorese person or resident in the national territory who practices acts suitable to assist or facilitate armed action or war that a foreign country or group conducts against Timor-Leste shall be punished with imprisonment from 10 to 20 years. Article 198 Sabotage of national defense

Anyone who, with the intention to prejudice or endanger national defense, destroys, damages or renders unusable, totally or partially:
a) Works or materials of the armed forces or works or materials allocated to the armed forces; b) Routes or means of communication or transport; c) Any installation related to communication or transport; or
d) Factories and depots,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. 107 (198) Allocated (adj) – That which has already been designated for. Allocated to the armed forces = that which has already been designated for the armed forces; that which the armed forces will use. Article 199
Campaign against peace efforts

A Timorese person or resident in the national territory who, in time of preparation for or conduct of war, disseminates, by any means, to the general public, rumors or statements, their own or another's, which they know to be false, totally or partially, in order to prejudice efforts for Timor-Leste's peace or to assist a foreign enemy, shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. Article 200
Violation of State secrets

1. Anyone who enables an unauthorized person to know or discloses to the public any facts, documents, plans, objects, knowledge or information which, due to the Timorese State's interest in external security or in the conduct of foreign policy, a foreign country should not know, thus endangering such interest, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 10 years. 2. Anyone who collaborates with a foreign government or group with the intention to commit the facts referred to in the previous number or recruits or assists persons who must commit such facts shall be punished with the penalty provided in the previous number. 3. When the agent who commits the facts described in the previous number exercises a political, public or military function which, by its nature, should prevent them from committing such facts more strongly than an ordinary citizen, imprisonment from 5 to 15 years shall be applied. Article 201 Diplomatic infidelity

Anyone who, officially representing the Timorese State, with the intention to prejudice national rights or interests:
a) Conducts negotiations concerning the State with a foreign government or international organization; or

b) Assumes commitments in the name of Timor-Leste without authorization to assume them,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. Article 202 Alteration of the lawful State

1. Anyone who, through violence, threat of violence or incitement to civil war, seeks to overthrow, change or dominate the lawful State established by the constitution shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. 2. Anyone who commits the act described in the previous number with armed violence must be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 20 years. 3. Anyone who makes public incitement or distributes weapons to anyone to commit the acts described above shall be punished, respectively, with the penalty for attempt. Article 203
Attack against the highest representative of a sovereign organ

1. Anyone who commits an attack against the highest representative of a sovereign organ or a person who, according to the constitution, substitutes them, or a person whose appointment has already been announced but who has not yet assumed the position, shall be punished with imprisonment from 8 to 20 years. 2. When a crime against life, physical integrity or liberty is consummated, the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 12 to 25 years. 3. The penalties provided in the previous numbers shall also apply to anyone who commits the acts already described against a foreign person who falls within the situation provided in number 1, ambassadors and persons serving as directing organs of international organizations, when they are residing in Timor-Leste. Article 204
Coercion against a constitutional organ

1. Anyone who, through violence or threat of violence, prevents or limits a sovereign organ from exercising its function freely shall be punished with imprisonment from 3 to 10 years. 2. Anyone who commits the act described in the previous number against an organ of district or local power shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years. 3. When someone commits the act described in number 1 against any member of those organs, the maximum and minimum limits of the penalties referred to in numbers 1 and 2 shall be reduced by half. Article 205
Disturbance of the functioning of constitutional organs

Anyone who through tumult, disorder or shouting illegitimately disturbs the functioning of the organs referred to in the previous numbers or anyone sitting in those organs to exercise their function shall be punished with imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine. Article 206
Outrage against national symbols

Anyone who publicly, by word, gesture or written dissemination or by other means of communication with the public, offends the national flag or anthem, the arms or emblem of Timorese sovereignty or shows disrespect due to those symbols shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. CHAPTER III AGAINST LIFE IN SOCIETY

SECTION I CRIMES OF COMMON DANGER

Article 207 Driving without a license

Anyone who uses a motorized vehicle without having the license required by law shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. Article 208
Driving under the influence of alcohol or psychotropic substances

1. Anyone who, at least through negligence, operates a motorized vehicle when they have 1.2sg of alcohol in each liter of their blood shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. 2. Anyone who, at least through negligence, operates a motorized vehicle when they are not in a condition to drive safely because they are under the influence of narcotics or under the influence of analogous products that cause physical, mental or psychological disturbance. Article 209 Dangerous driving

1. Anyone who operates a motorized vehicle on a public road and, due to not being in a condition to drive safely or due to grossly violating road traffic rules, endangers another person's life or physical integrity shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 4 years. 2. Negligence regarding the conduct or danger shall give rise to imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. Article 210
Attack on transport safety

1. Anyone who commits acts suitable to destroy or reduce safety in means of transport and, thus, endangers another person's life or physical integrity shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 2. Number 2 of the previous article also applies here. Article 211 Prohibited weapons

1. Anyone who, in situations not authorized by law, manufactures, imports, transports, sells or gives to another person firearms, chemical weapons, biological weapons, nuclear weapons, ammunition for such weapons, substances to manufacture such weapons or for them to function or other types of explosives shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years. 2. When the facts described in the previous number have the purpose of using such weapons in war, imprisonment from 2 to 8 years shall be applied. 3. An agent who, without legal authorization, possesses, uses or carries a firearm shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. Article 212 Drunkenness and intoxication

1. Anyone who, at least through negligence, renders themselves non-imputable due to drinking or consuming alcoholic beverages or toxic substances and in that state commits some typical illicit act shall be punished with imprisonment up to 5 years or a fine. 2. The penalty may not be heavier than that provided for the typical illicit act that the agent committed. 3. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 213
Qualification to exercise certain activities

1. Anyone who, without legal qualification, sells, administers, prescribes or gives, in any way, to another person, habitually, pharmaceutical or other products whose commerce and prescription, administration and dispensation only health professionals or entities with due license may do shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the facts referred to in the previous number endanger another person's life, imprisonment from 1 to 4 years shall be applied. Article 214
Adulterated or deteriorated products

1. Anyone who places for people to purchase, administers or gives, in any way, food, pharmaceutical or other products which, due to being deteriorated, adulterated or contaminated,

may endanger life shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 2. When the facts described in the previous number cause the victim's death due to their consumption of such products, the penalty is from 3 to 12 years. SECTION II
CRIMES AGAINST THE ENVIRONMENT

Article 215 Against the environment

1. Anyone who, contrary to legal or regulatory provisions protecting the environment, provokes or causes, directly or indirectly, emission, discharge, radiation, extraction or excavation, landfilling, noise, vibration, injection or deposit in the atmosphere, in soil, subsoil or terrestrial, maritime or underground water, including in border zones, or water capture, which may seriously prejudice the equilibrium of natural systems, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the agent deliberately releases, emits or introduces ionizing radiation or other substances into the air, land or maritime, continental, surface or underground water, in quantities that cause one or more persons to suffer serious bodily harm requiring medical or surgical treatment or cause irreversible sequelae, they shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years, and, if they cause someone's death, they shall be punished with imprisonment from 5 to 15 years. Article 216 Aggravation

1. When an industrial or commercial establishment commits the activities provided in the previous article and any of the following circumstances exist:
a) The industry or commercial activity operates clandestinely without license or administrative authorization; b) Does not comply with the express order of the competent administrative authority to correct or suspend the activity referred to in the previous article; c) Does not comply with safety rules or procedures provided in legal or regulatory provisions; d) By any means deliberately prevents the inspection process of the competent administrative authority or falsifies information about the consequences of the industry or commercial activity; e) Produces irreversible or catastrophic environmental deterioration,
the maximum and minimum limits of the penalty provided in the previous number shall be increased by one third. 2. Criminal responsibility shall also be borne, under the previous number, by the person sitting as individual holder, the person who legally represents the collective person that owns the offending industrial or commercial establishment, the person who acts as the legal representative of that collective person, and also, in irregularly constituted societies, the partners or members who authorized those actions. Article 217 Against flora and fauna

1. Anyone who, with serious prejudice to the environment, cuts, burns, poisons, collects or illegally traffics in flora species classified as threatened or in danger of extinction, or their seeds, due to destroying or seriously altering their natural habitat, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. The penalty provided in the previous number shall also apply to anyone who introduces or facilitates entry of non-autochthonous species and, thus, prejudices biological equilibrium, contrary to law or provisions of general character to protect fauna or flora species. Article 218
Threatened or endangered species

1. Anyone who hunts or fishes threatened species or practices activities that prevent the development of such species or create difficulties for their reproduction or migration, contrary to law or provisions of general character to protect wild fauna species, or commerces or traffics in such species, totally or partially, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. An agent who commits the acts referred to in the previous number:
a) In a terrestrial or maritime zone declared a protected natural zone; b) Against species or subspecies classified as in danger of extinction
shall be punished with imprisonment up to 5 years or a fine. Article 219 Illegal fishing

1. Anyone who practices fishing in national maritime waters without a fishing license from the competent administrative authority shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the agent is a collective person, criminal responsibility shall be borne, under the previous number, by the person who, according to law, represents that collective person, the person who acts as the legal representative of that collective person, and also, in irregularly constituted societies, the partners or members who authorized those actions. 3. Fishing for domestic subsistence is not punished under number 1. Article 220 Illicit fishing means

Anyone who uses firearms, explosives, toxic substances or other instruments or similar arts with destructive efficacy against marine fauna to capture fishery resources in national terrestrial or maritime waters shall be punished with imprisonment up to 5 years or a fine. Article 221

Prohibited burning
1. Anyone who, in a season not for burning or without necessary administrative authorization, conducts burning that causes destruction of forests, plantations or crops shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. 2. An agent who conducts legal burning but, through negligence, provokes the prejudice referred to in the previous number shall be punished with imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine. SECTION III OTHER CRIMES

Article 222
Impediment or disturbance of procession, ceremony or worship

1. Anyone who, through violence or threat of violence or any coercion, prevents or disturbs the conduct of a funeral procession or ceremony or the exercise of religious worship shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. 2. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 223
Profanation of objects or places of worship or veneration

1. Anyone who, in order to cause alarm or disturb social peace, profanes a place or object of religious worship or veneration shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. 2. Anyone who offends or insults another person because of their faith or religious function, in a manner suitable to cause social alarm or disturbance shall be punished with the same penalty. 3. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 224

108 (223) Alarm (n) – Shock; fear
109 (223) Profane (v) - violate something or a place that is sacred
110 (223) Veneration (n) – Adoration; behavior that shows great respect
111 (223) Disturbance (n) – Confusion; instability

Destruction, subtraction, concealment or profanation of corpse

1. Anyone who subtracts, destroys or conceals a corpse or any human remains or cremated remains, contrary to the will of the person having the right or without that person's will and in situations not authorized by law, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. 2. Anyone who profanes a corpse or any human remains or cremated remains through acts that show disrespect for the deceased shall be punished with the same penalty. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 4. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 225
Failure to fulfill alimentary obligation

1. Anyone who has an obligation to provide alimony and is in a condition to fulfill that obligation but does not fulfill that obligation to the point of endangering the alimentary recipient's ability to satisfy their fundamental needs, even though another person's assistance has removed that danger, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 226 Subtraction of minor

1. Anyone who subtracts or refuses to deliver a minor to the person who has assumed the function of caring for that minor or causes that minor to run away shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. If such facts are done with violence or threat of violence, imprisonment from 1 to 4 years shall be applied. 3. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 227 Omission of assistance

112 (225) Alimony (n) – Food, clothing, place to live, education, treatment and medicine for health that according to law a person must provide to their child or their spouse or others

1. Anyone who, in a time of serious need, namely due to disaster, accident, public calamity or common danger, which endangers another person's life, physical integrity or liberty, does not provide necessary assistance to remove that danger, through personal action or promotion of assistance, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine. 2. When the person who omits necessary assistance is the one who caused the situation provided in the previous number, that omitter shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. 3. The omission of assistance does not give rise to penalty when there is serious danger to the omitter's life or physical integrity or when, due to other relevant reason, such assistance cannot be required of them. 4. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 228 Refusal of medical assistance

A doctor or health professional who refuses assistance relating to their profession in a situation where there is danger to another person's life or serious danger to another person's physical integrity, which there is no other way to remove, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. CHAPTER IV ELECTORAL CRIMES

Article 229 Fraud in census

1. Anyone who prevents another person, whom they know has the right, from registering themselves, inserts facts they know to be false, omits facts they must inscribe or, by any means, falsifies the electoral census shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 113 (227) Relevant (adj) – Important; heavy; strong
114 (228) Refuse (v) – Unwilling to give or do (something)
115 (228) Physical integrity – Complete body, which is not injured or sick

2. When the agent uses violence or provokes cunning deception to prevent someone from registering themselves, imprisonment from 2 to 6 years shall be applied. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 230 Obstruction of candidacy

Anyone who, by any means, prevents another person, party or political force, whom they know has the right, from competing in an electoral act shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. Article 231 Ineligible candidate

1. Anyone who presents their candidacy when they know they do not have the capacity to be elected shall be punished with imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 232
Deficiency in electoral register

Anyone who, charged with elaborating the electoral register or guaranteeing its correctness, does not elaborate or does not make that correction or prevents their legal substitute from doing so, thereby preventing the electoral act from being held, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. Article 233 Illicit electoral propaganda

116 (229) Deception (n) – Mistaken belief. Cunning deception = Mistaken belief through lies
117 (229) Cunning (adj) – That which uses lies
118 (229) Register themselves (v) – Put name; make their inscription. 119 (232) Electoral register – A list with the names of people who can vote
120 (232) Correctness (n) – Correct. Guarantee the correctness of the electoral register
= guarantee the electoral register has nothing wrong. Make correction = Correct; fix; make correct. 1. Anyone who uses means of propaganda prohibited by law or continues propaganda after the established deadline has passed, or conducts propaganda in a prohibited place shall be punished with imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine. 2. Anyone who prevents another person from exercising their right to conduct electoral propaganda or illegitimately destroys electoral propaganda shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. Article 234 Obstruction of freedom to choose

1. Anyone who through violence, threat of violence or fraudulent artifice compels another person, against their will, not to vote or to vote in a determined direction or buys or sells a vote shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. The same penalty shall also apply to anyone who, charged with assisting a blind person or a person who has the right to such assistance, does not respect the direction of the vote communicated to them. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 235 Disturbance of electoral act

1. Anyone who, by any means, disturbs the functioning of a voting assembly shall be punished with imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine. 2. The agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years when they cause that disturbance:
a) Through violence or threat of violence; b) Through tumult or popular gathering near the voting assembly; c) Through intentional cutting of electrical energy; 121 (234) Artifice (n) – Means; device; mechanism
122 (234) Determined (adj) – One direction that is clearly indicated. Determined direction = one direction that is clearly indicated. 123 (234) Charged (v) - Has received an obligation or position; has received some work to do. Charged with assisting = has received the obligation to assist. d) Through the absence of persons indispensable for that act when that absence creates great difficulty for the electoral act to begin or continue. 3. The provisions of the previous numbers shall also apply to an agent who commits such acts when, after voting has finished, the counting of results is being conducted. Article 236
Obstruction of supervision of electoral act

1. Anyone who, in any way, prevents the representative of a legally constituted party or political force competing in the electoral act from exercising their competence to conduct supervision shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 237 Fraud in voting

1. Anyone who votes despite not having the right to vote or votes repeatedly despite having already voted in that electoral act shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Anyone who deliberately allows someone to commit the acts provided in the previous number shall be punished with the same penalty. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 238 Fraud in scrutiny

1. Anyone who, in any way, falsifies the counting of votes in the act of ascertaining or publishing electoral results shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years. 2. Anyone who, with fraudulent intention, substitutes, destroys, suppresses, violates,

124 (235) Indispensable (adj) – That which is truly needed; that which cannot be left out. 125 (236) Competing (n) – That which has entered as a candidate in the election
126 (238) Counting (n) – Act or activity of counting

views or falsifies a ballot or count or document relating to the election shall be punished with the same penalty. Article 239 Refusal of electoral position

Anyone who, having received appointment to sit at the table of a voting assembly and, unjustifiably, refuses to assume or abandons that function shall be punished with imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine. Article 240
Violation of secrecy in scrutiny

Anyone who in an electoral act with secret scrutiny violates secrecy by taking or disclosing knowledge about whom another person voted for shall be punished with imprisonment up to 1 year or a fine. Article 241
Duty of neutrality or impartiality

A public official in electoral administration or a collaborator of electoral administration who violates their duty of neutrality and impartiality shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. Article 242 Aggravation

When anyone who commits any crime provided in this chapter performs a public function, namely in government, national parliament, or armed forces, as a judicial magistrate or in the Public Prosecutor's Office, in police forces or other administrative organs, the penalty provided in each type shall rise to double in its minimum and maximum limits. CHAPTER V

127 Scrutiny (n) – Voting; activity to know how many votes each candidate received

AGAINST PUBLIC AUTHORITY
Article 243 Obstruction of public authority

1. Anyone who, through violence or serious threat against a public official or agent of military, militarized or police forces, does not allow them to practice an act relating to their function or makes them practice an act contrary to their duty shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years. 2. When the act referred to in the previous number is actually done or actually prevented, imprisonment from 2 to 8 years shall be applied. Article 244 Disobedience

1. Anyone who does not comply or continues not to comply with a legitimate order or mandate regularly communicated, which a competent authority or official gives, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine:
a) When a legal provision so determines; or
b) When that authority or official informs the agent that, if they do not comply with that order or mandate, they will incur criminal responsibility. 2. In situations where a legal provision qualifies an act as qualified disobedience, imprisonment from 1 to 4 years shall be applied. 3. Disobedience to a specific prohibition or interdiction that a criminal sentence determines and also disobedience to an accessory penalty or security measure that does not deprive liberty shall give rise to the penalty referred to in number 1. Article 245 Freeing prisoner
1. Anyone who, by illegal means, frees a person who has legally lost their liberty or, by any means, helps that person escape shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years. 2. When such acts are committed with violence, weapons or with the collaboration of more than two people, imprisonment from 2 to 8 years shall be applied. Article 246 Evasion

1. Anyone who, according to law, has lost their liberty and escapes shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 4 years. 2. When evasion is achieved by the means described in the previous article, number 2, imprisonment from 2 to 6 years shall be applied. Article 247 Official's assistance in evasion

1. An official who assists someone to commit the acts provided in the previous articles shall be punished with the penalty established therein plus one third in its minimum and maximum limits. 2. When the official who has the obligation to hold or conduct surveillance of the evader gives assistance to commit such acts, the maximum and minimum limits of the provided penalties shall be further increased by half. 3. When evasion arises due to gross negligence of the official who must watch the evader, imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine shall be applied. Article 248 Prisoners' mutiny

1. Persons who, having lost their liberty according to law, having already conspired and combining their efforts with persons in circumstances similar to theirs, attack or threaten with violence the person who has the obligation to watch or guard them, in order to achieve their evasion or another person's evasion or compel that person to practice some act or not practice some act, shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 2. When through the behaviors described in the previous number some of the agents or some other persons actually escape, imprisonment from 3 to 10 years shall be applied. Article 249
Diversion or destruction of objects in public power's hands

Anyone who destroys, damages or renders unusable, totally or partially, or in any way, subtracts from public power that has received or controls documents or other movable objects, or things arrested, seized or objects under precautionary provision shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years, when another norm does not give rise to a heavier penalty. Article 250
Breaking of mark, seal or edict

1. Anyone who opens, tears or renders unusable, totally or partially, a mark or seal, which a competent official has legitimately placed to identify or make inviolable something or to certify that arrest, seizure or precautionary provision has already fallen upon something, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Anyone who, in any way, intentionally, prevents the addressee from knowing an edict that a competent official has posted shall be punished with imprisonment up to 2 years or a fine. TITLE IV
CRIMES AGAINST PATRIMONY

CHAPTER I AGAINST PROPERTY

Article 251 Simple theft

1. Anyone who, with illegitimate intention to make their own or another's, subtracts another person's movable thing shall be punished with imprisonment up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 3. To conduct criminal proceedings, a complaint is required. Article 252 Aggravated theft

1. An agent who commits the acts referred to in the previous article shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years, when:
a) That movable thing of another person has great scientific, artistic or historical value or is something important for economic or technological development; b) That movable thing of another person is a vehicle, which a vehicle or passenger in a collective vehicle is carrying, or is held at a dock or station for embarkation or disembarkation; c) That movable thing of another person is something to be used in religious worship or in veneration of the memory of the deceased and is held in a place for worship or in a cemetery; d) The agent takes advantage of night to commit that theft more easily; e) The victim suffers considerable prejudice due to that appropriation; f) The agent enters a dwelling, public service installation, commercial or industrial establishment with the intention to commit theft; g) The agent uses a false key, scaling or breaking to realize their intention; h) The agent takes advantage of the victim's special vulnerability in disaster, accident or public calamity; i) The agent takes advantage of special trust arising from their relationship with the victim or with the place where the subtracted thing is held by its owner; j) The agent commits theft as their daily work; k) Three or more persons, including the agent, commit that crime; l) The subtracted thing has a value exceeding US$
1,000 but not exceeding $5,000. 128 (252) Commits theft as their daily work = Commits theft as a way of life. 129 (252) Including (v) – Counted with. Three persons, including the agent = three persons, counted with the agent. 2. When the subtracted thing has a value exceeding US$5,000, imprisonment from 3 to 10 years shall be applied. 3. When several circumstances referred to in number 1 characterize one conduct, only the circumstance that has the strongest aggravating effect shall be considered to determine the abstract framework of the applicable legal type and the other circumstances shall be valued as circumstances of general character to determine the measure of the penalty. 4. When the stolen thing has a value less than US$50, the circumstances written in number 1 function only as aggravating circumstances of general character. Article 253 Robbery

1. Anyone who, with illegitimate intention to make their own or another's, subtracts or compels delivery to them of another person's movable thing, through violence against a person, threat of imminent danger to a person's life or to a person's physical integrity, or places that person in a situation where they cannot resist, shall be punished with imprisonment from 3 to 10 years. 2. When, together with the agent's conduct, there also arises any circumstance written in the previous article, number 1, imprisonment from 4 to 12 years shall be applied. 3.3. When the conduct causes danger to the victim's life or serious offense to the victim's physical integrity, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of 5 to 15 years. 4. When the conduct causes the death of someone, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of 5 to 20 years. Article 254 Violence following subtraction

A person who, after someone has already committed theft, acts as described in the previous article in order to retain the appropriated thing or prevent the appropriated thing's

130 (253) Eminent (adj) - That which is about to occur soon

restitution shall receive the penalties established for the crime of robbery, respectively. Article 255
Theft for vehicle use

1. A person who uses an automobile or motorized vehicle, aircraft, boat or bicycle without the authorization of the person who has the right shall receive a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 3. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 256 Abuse of trust

1. A person who, illegitimately, makes their own a movable thing that was delivered to them with a title131 that does not transfer ownership shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 3. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 257
Aggravated abuse of trust

1. When the movable thing has a value higher than US$1,000, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. 2. The penalty described in the previous number and in article 256 shall have its minimum and maximum limits increased by one third when the agent received the thing for deposit by order of law, by reason of their office, employment or profession or as guardian, curator or depositary. Article 258 Simple damage

1. A person who, totally or partially, destroys, damages or renders useless another person's thing shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 131 (256) Title (n) – Quality; basis

2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 3. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 259 Aggravated damage

1. A person who, totally or partially, destroys, damages or renders useless a thing:
a) That is for public use or has public utility; b) With important scientific, artistic or historical value or with great importance for technological or scientific development; c) Concerning means of communication or transport with great social importance; d) Thus causing damage higher than US$1,000; e) Belonging to another person, that is to be used in religious worship or to venerate the memory of the dead and kept in a place for worship or in a cemetery,
shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. Article 260 Damage with violence

When the agent commits the acts described in articles 258 and 259 with violence against someone or with threat of imminent danger to life or physical integrity, or by placing that person in a situation where they cannot resist, a prison sentence of 4 to 12 years shall be applied. Article 261 Usurpation of immovable property

1. A person who, with violence or serious threat against another person, invades or occupies another person's immovable property, with the intention of exercising a right of ownership, possession, use or easement that the law, judgment, contract or administrative act does not protect132, shall receive a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years. 132 (261) Protect (v) - To safeguard

2. When the means the agent uses constitutes a crime punishable with a heavier penalty than that which the previous number imposes133, that penalty shall be applied. Article 262 Alteration of boundary markers

1. A person who, with the intention of making their own or another person's, totally or partially, another person's immovable property, removes or alters a boundary marker or any sign that serves to establish limits concerning ownership of immovable property shall receive a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine. 2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 263 Arson

1. A person who, intentionally, causes a fire in a house, building, establishment, means of transport, forest, plantation or other property and, thus, causes danger to life, physical integrity or patrimonial property with a value higher than US$5,000 shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. 2. For the acts described in the previous number concerning public property or property in which a public service operates, a prison sentence of 2 to 10 years shall be applied. 3. If the agent commits these acts through negligence, they shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine, when another legal provision does not provide for a heavier penalty. 4. When only the danger referred to in number 1 arises through negligence, a penalty of 2 to 6 years shall be applied. 5. In the cases that number 4 provides for, there must be a complaint to carry out criminal proceedings. Article 264 Active repentance

When, having committed the crimes provided for in articles 251, 252, 256 to 261 and 263,
no. 4, but, before the hearing for judgment begins, the agent commits some act to repair,

133 (261) Impose (v) – To provide for; to establish

integrally or partially, the damage they caused, the penalty may receive extraordinary mitigation. Article 265 Definition

For the purposes of this code's provisions, the following shall be considered:
a) "Breaking in" the breaking134, fracture or destruction, total or partial, of a device that serves to close or prevent entry, from outside or inside, to a house or to a closed place that depends on that house; b) "Climbing" entry into a house or closed place that depends on that house, through a place not ordinarily used as an entrance or through a device to close or prevent entry or passage; c) False key
i) An imitated, counterfeited or altered key; ii) A true key when, fortuitously135 or surreptitiously, it leaves the hands of the person who has the right to use it; and
iii) A picklock136 or instrument that can be used to open a lock or other security device. CHAPTER II
AGAINST PATRIMONY IN GENERAL

Article 266 Simple fraud

1. A person who, with the intention of obtaining illegitimate enrichment for themselves or for another person, through error or deception concerning a fact that they astutely provoke, causes another person to commit an act that causes patrimonial damage to that person or to another person shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 134 (256) Breaking (n) – Act that breaks something
135 (265) Fortuitously (adv) – Casually; accidentally
136 (265) Picklock (n) – Metal tool to open locks or padlocks

3. The provision of article 264 also applies here. 4. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 267 Aggravated fraud

1. A person who, through their conduct described in the previous article:
a) Causes damage with a value higher than US$2,000; b) Commits fraud as their daily occupation; c) Places the victim in a difficult economic situation,
shall receive a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years. 2. The provision of article 264 also applies here. Article 268 Computer fraud

1. A person who, with the intention of obtaining illegitimate enrichment for themselves or for another person, causes patrimonial damage to another person, by interfering with the result of computer data processing or by making incorrect structuring of a computer program, not using data correctly or completely, using data despite lacking authorization or intervening in any way in processing despite lacking authorization, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 3. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 269
Aggravated computer fraud

When the conduct described in the previous article causes any circumstance described in article 267,
no. 1, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years. Article 270 Extortion

1. A person who, with the intention of obtaining for themselves or for another person illegitimate enrichment, compels another person, with violence or threat of important harm, to make a patrimonial disposition137 that causes damage to that person or to another person, shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years. 2. When any circumstance provided for in article 252 arises, the agent shall receive the penalty provided for in that article. 3. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 271 Simple receiving

1. A person who, before seeking to know whether it has legitimate provenance or not, acquires or receives, with any title, a thing that, according to its nature or according to the person who has, holds or offers it, or according to its price or the conditions of its sale or offer, a person with ordinary care has reason to suspect that the thing comes from criminal conduct against another person's patrimony shall receive a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine penalty. 2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 272 Aggravated receiving

1. A person who, with the intention of obtaining patrimonial advantage for themselves or for another person, hides a thing that another person obtained through a crime against patrimony, receives, gives as pledge, makes their own with any title, holds, keeps, delivers or contributes to delivering the thing to another person, or in any way seeks a way for the thing or its value or product that comes directly from the thing to remain in their hands or in another person's hands shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. 2. The agent who commits the acts described in the previous number shall receive a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years when:

137 (270) Patrimonial disposition – a person makes a patrimonial disposition when they deliver to another person some patrimonial property. a) They commit receiving as their daily occupation to live or habitually; b) The property, value or product has a value138 higher than US$2,000; c) At the time of committing the receiving they knew that the crime was committed in any circumstance described in article 253,
no. 1. Article 273 Assistance to criminal

1. A person who, after a crime against patrimony, helps the agent of that crime to benefit from the thing they obtained in that way or the benefit that comes directly from the appropriated thing shall receive a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine. 2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 274 Harmful administration

1. A person who, when having an obligation to manage or administer another person's interest, service or patrimonial property, even if they are also a partner of the company or collective person that owns the interest, service or property, causes economically significant patrimonial damage by intentionally violating rules concerning control or management or acting with serious violation of the duty concerning their function, shall receive a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years. 2. If the property, interest or service referred to in the previous number belongs to the State, a collective person with public utility, a cooperative or a popular association, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years. 3. These penalties shall also be applied to a person who makes their own or lets another person make their own, illegitimately, a thing that they can manage only according to the purpose that those who administer another person's patrimony must follow. Article 275

138 (272) Value (n) - Price

Negligent administration
1. A person who, in the conditions described in the previous article's number 1, causes patrimonial damage because they do not act with the care that, according to the circumstances surrounding their conduct and as they are able, they have an obligation to use, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine. 2. If the property or interest is that described in the previous article, number 2, a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine shall be applied. 3. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. Article 276
Intentional bankruptcy or insolvency

1. A person who, by any means, leads some company to bankruptcy or places it in insolvency, with the intention of harming creditors, when that bankruptcy or insolvency receives a declaration, shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. 2. If the fact described in the previous number concerns a public enterprise or cooperative, the penalty's minimum or maximum limit shall be aggravated by one third. Article 277
Negligent bankruptcy or insolvency

1. A person who causes bankruptcy139 or insolvency140 through serious carelessness141 or imprudence142, manifest prodigality143 or exaggerated expense, or through gross negligence in their activity, when that bankruptcy or insolvency receives a declaration, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine. 139 (277) Bankruptcy (n) – Situation in which some merchant or commercial company enters when they cannot fulfill their obligation because their debt is greater than their patrimony
140 (277) Insolvency (n) – Situation in which someone enters when they cannot pay their debt because their patrimony is not enough to pay. 141 (277) Carelessness (n) – Lack of care
142 (277) Imprudence (n) – Lack of care; careless act; not thinking beforehand (before doing something)
143 (277) Prodigality (n) – Quality of a person who spends wastefully

2. To carry out criminal proceedings there must be a complaint. TITLE V
CRIMES AGAINST THE REALIZATION OF JUSTICE

Article 278
Falsity in testimony or declaration

1. A person who, in testimony as a party, having taken an oath and having received a warning about the penal consequences that will fall upon them for false testimony, gives a false declaration concerning a fact about which they must give testimony shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. A suspect or accused shall receive the same penalty here for a declaration concerning their identity and criminal record. 3. When, due to the conduct described in the previous numbers, someone loses their freedom, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. Article 279
Falsity in testimony, expertise, interpretation or translation

1. A person who, as a witness, expert, technician, translator or interpreter before a tribunal or before a competent official to receive as a means of proof testimony, report, information or translation, gives testimony, presents a report, gives information or makes a false translation shall receive a prison sentence of up to 4 years or a fine. 2. A person who refuses to give testimony or present a report, information or translation when they have no just cause144 shall receive the same penalty. 3. When, having taken an oath and having received a warning about the penal consequences that will fall upon them, the agent commits the fact referred to in no. 1, a prison sentence of up to 5 years shall be applied. 144 (279) Cause (n) – Motive. Just cause – Motive that justifies someone not committing some act or not committing the act within the deadline. 4. When the conduct described in the previous numbers causes someone to lose their freedom, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. Article 280 Equivalence to desistance

The repentance and retraction of the agent who commits some fact described in the previous article before the falsity's effect receives consideration in a decision or the falsity causes damage to another person is equivalent to desistance. Article 281 Bribery

1. A person who convinces145 or attempts146 to convince another person, with a present or promise concerning patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage, to commit the fact referred to in article 278 or 279 and that person indeed commits that fact, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 4 years. 2. When the fact that articles 278 and 279 refer to is not actually committed, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. Article 282 Denial of justice

1. An official who in a procedural inquiry, jurisdictional process, process for counter-order or disciplinary matter, knowingly, decides or does not decide, promotes or does not promote, investigates or does not investigate or commits an act in their function, against the law, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the official commits the fact described in the previous number with the intention of harming or benefiting someone, they shall receive a prison sentence of up to 5 years. 145 (281) Convince (v) – To use strong reasoned argument or logical argument to make another person follow our thinking; to persuade; to lead; to induce. 146 (281) Attempt to convince (v) – To pressure someone to induce them to do something; to insist in order to do or obtain something. 3. When the conduct that the previous numbers describe causes someone to lose their freedom, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. 4. When the agent commits the previous conduct through gross negligence, the penalty's minimum and maximum limit shall be reduced to half. Article 283 Coercion of magistrate

1. A person who, with violence, threat of important harm or by any means prevents a judge or public prosecutor from freely exercising their function shall receive a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years. 2. When, to commit these acts, the agent uses a political, public, military or police position that they hold, a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years shall be applied. 3. When, due to the conduct described in the previous numbers, the magistrate omits or commits some act against express law and that act causes damage to another person, a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years shall be applied. Article 284
Obstruction of jurisdictional activity

1. A person who, by any means, makes opposition, causes difficulty or blocks a judicial decision with res judicata from being fulfilled or executed shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 5 years. 2. When the agent who commits the acts provided for in the previous numbers exercises a political, public or military function that, according to its nature, should block them from committing these acts, they shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. Article 285 Slanderous denunciation

1. A person who, by any means, before an authority or publicly, with awareness that the imputation is false, denounces or spreads suspicion that someone committed a crime

with the intention of subjecting that person to criminal proceedings shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the agent makes a false imputation concerning a counter-order or disciplinary illicit, the penalty shall receive extraordinary mitigation. 3. When an official who has an obligation to carry out criminal proceedings fraudulently promotes the fact described in the previous numbers, the applicable penalty's minimum and maximum limit shall be aggravated by one third. Article 286 Non-participation

A person who, having had knowledge that someone committed some public crime and having an obligation to make a report, does not report shall receive a penalty concerning that crime which they concealed with its minimum and maximum limit reduced by two thirds. Article 287
Prevarication of magistrate or official

1. A judge, public prosecutor or official who in any phase of a jurisdictional process, with the intention of benefiting or harming another person, commits some act that falls within their functional power or does not commit an act that they have an obligation to commit shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years. 2. When the fact described in the previous number causes someone to lose their freedom or causes illegal imprisonment or detention, a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years shall be applied. Article 288
Prevarication of lawyer or public defender

1. A lawyer or public defender who intentionally harms a case that is entrusted to their patronage shall receive a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years. 2. A lawyer or public defender who, in a single case, advocates or exercises defense for persons whose interests are in conflict, with the intention of benefiting or

harming some of them, shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years. Article 289 Simulation of crime

1. A person who, without saying who committed it, denounces a crime or makes a competent authority suspect that someone committed some crime, when they know that there is no crime, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine. 2. If this fact concerns a contravention, counter-order or disciplinary illicit, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine. 3. When an official who has an obligation to institute proceedings against the facts described in the previous numbers commits these facts, the applicable penalty's minimum and maximum limit shall be aggravated by one third. Article 290 Personal favoritism

1. A person who, totally or partially, prevents, frustrates147 or eludes the authority's probative or preventive activity, with the intention or with awareness of preventing a person who committed a crime from receiving a penalty or security measure, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Attempt gives rise to punishment. 3. When an official who intervenes or has competence to intervene in a process or has an obligation to execute a penalty or security measure or to order its execution commits this favoritism, a prison sentence of 2 to 5 years shall be applied. 4. The acts described in number 1 do not give rise to penalty when:
a) The agent commits the fact to also prevent a penalty or security measure from being applied or executed against them; b) The person whom the agent wants to favor with this conduct is the agent's spouse, descendant, ascendant, adopter, adoptee or relative or affine up to

147 (290) Frustrate – to make something not produce its result or effect; to make something fail

the second degree, or a person who lives with them as husband and wife. Article 291
Violation of justice's secrecy

1. A person who, against legal determination and without just cause, releases to the public the content of a penal procedural act that falls within justice's secrecy or an act whose publicity a decision has already prohibited, shall receive a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years. 2. When the agent commits this violation through means of social communication, a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years shall be applied. TITLE VI
CRIMES IN THE EXERCISE OF PUBLIC FUNCTION

Article 292 Passive corruption for illicit act

1. An official who, themselves or through another person, with their authorization or ratification, requests or receives, for themselves or for another person, patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage or the promise of that advantage, even though they have no right to it, to commit or not commit some act against the obligation they have due to the position they hold, even if that act or omission already occurred before the request or acceptance arose, shall receive a prison sentence of 3 to 15 years. 2. When the agent, before committing this fact, voluntarily refuses the offer or promise that they already received or returns the advantage, or, if the advantage is a fungible thing, its value, they shall not receive a penalty. 3. The penalty shall receive special mitigation when the agent indeed helps to obtain decisive proof to identify or capture other responsible persons. Article 293 Passive corruption for licit act

1. An official who, themselves or through another person, with their authorization or ratification, requests or receives, for themselves or for another person, patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage or the promise of that advantage, even though they have no right to it, to commit or not commit some act not against the obligation they have due to the position they hold, even if that act or omission already occurred before the request or acceptance arose, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. An official who, themselves or through another person, with their authorization or ratification, requests or receives, for themselves or for another person, patrimonial or non-patrimonial advantage or the promise of that advantage, even though they have no right to it, from a person who already requested, will soon request or will later request something that they must exercise the function they hold for that person to obtain, shall receive the same penalty. Article 294 Active corruption

1. A person who, themselves or through another person, with their authorization or ratification, gives or promises to an official or to another person with that official's knowledge, patrimonial advantage that the official has no right to receive, with the purpose indicated in article 292, shall receive a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years. 2. When through the conduct described in the previous number the agent wants to obtain the purpose that article 293 indicates, they shall receive a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine. Article 295 Embezzlement

1. An official who, against the law, makes their own or makes another person's money or movable thing, public or private, that is entrusted to them or comes into their hands or they can hold due to their function, shall receive a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years, when another legal provision does not order the application of a heavier penalty. 2. An official who lends, pawns or, in any way, encumbers the value or object that the previous number concerns shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine, when another legal provision does not order the application of a heavier penalty. 3. When the money or movable things that the previous numbers concern have a value higher than US$5,000, a prison sentence of 4 to 12 years or 2 to 5 years shall be applied, respectively. 4. When the money or movable things that number 1 concerns have a value lower than US$50, a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine shall be applied. Article 296 Embezzlement by use

An official who uses or lets another person use for a purpose that does not fall within its destination a vehicle or other movable thing with significant value, that comes into their hands or they can hold due to their function, to obtain for themselves or for another person illegitimate benefit or cause damage to someone, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 2 years, when these things come into their hands or they can hold them due to their function. Article 297 Abuse of power

An official who abuses power or violates an obligation, that comes from the function they hold, to obtain benefit for themselves or for another person or cause damage to another person shall receive a prison sentence of 1 to 4 years, when another legal provision does not order the application of a heavier penalty. Article 298 Abuse with public force

An official who, having competence to use, requisition or order the use of public force, uses, requisitions or orders the use of that force to prevent the execution of law, a regular mandate of justice or a legitimate order of public authority

shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years, when another legal provision does not order the application of a heavier penalty. Article 299 Economic participation

1. An official who must intervene in a contract or operation or other activity, due to the public position they hold, and takes advantage of that condition to obtain for themselves or for another person, directly or through another person, patrimonial advantage or, in any way, illicit economic participation, and, thus, causes damage to the public interest that they must administer, supervise, defend or realize, shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. 2. When this conduct causes damage to the State higher than US$10,000, a prison sentence of 3 to 15 years shall be applied. Article 300 Refusal of cooperation

An official who, having received a request from a competent authority to give cooperation to administer justice or for another public service, and refuses that cooperation or, despite having no justified motive, does not give that cooperation, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. Article 301 Aggravation

1. When the holder of a political position or some magistrate commits the crimes that this title refers to, the respective penalty's maximum limit shall increase by one third. 2. Special legislation shall fix non-penal effects and procedure concerning the situations referred to in the previous number. Article 302 Concept of official
1. For the purposes of penal law, the expression official includes:

a) Civil official; b) Administrative agent; c) Members of the armed and police forces; d) A person who, even if provisionally or temporarily, with remuneration or gratuitously148, voluntarily or obligatorily, receives a function to hold or hold with another person an activity in administrative or jurisdictional public function, or, in similar circumstances, holds or holds with another person a function in an organism with public utility or participates in these functions. 2. For the purposes of penal law, the provisions of this title shall also be applied to a person who exercises a political, governmental or legislative function

TITLE VII FALSIFICATION CRIMES

CHAPTER I FALSIFICATION OF DOCUMENT

Article 303
Falsification of document or technical notation

1. A person who, to cause damage to another person or to the State or to obtain illegitimate benefit for themselves or for another person:
a) Manufactures a false document or technical notation, falsifies or alters a document or abuses another person's signature to make a false document; b) Falsely149 inserts into a document or technical notation a juridically relevant fact; c) Falsely attests150 concerning a person, animal or thing's physical or psychic state or quality, based on scientific knowledge; 148 (302) Gratuitously (adv) – For free. 149 (303) Falsely (adv) – In a false manner
150 (303) Attest (v) - To declare to make someone believe; to certify. d) Uses a document or technical notation referred to in the previous paragraphs, that another person manufactured, falsified or issued
shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Falsification of technical notation shall be considered as an action that causes disturbance to a technical or automatic apparatus used to influence the result of technical notation. 3. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 304 Aggravated falsification

1. When the facts referred to in the previous article,
no. 1, concern an authentic document or document with the same force as an authentic document, sealed will, money order, bill of exchange, check, other document transmissible by endorsement or technical notation concerning the identification, total or partial, of an automobile vehicle, aircraft or boat, the agent shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years. 2. If an official commits the facts described in the previous article or in article 309, number 1, when they are currently holding their function, a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years shall be applied. Article 305
Falsification of public document

An official who, in the function they hold, to cause damage to another person or to the State or to obtain illegitimate benefit for themselves or for another person:
a) Omits a fact that the document to which the law gives public faith must certify or authenticate; or
b) Intercalates an act or document in a protocol, register or official book not following legal formality
shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years. Article 306
Use of another's identification document

A person who, with the intention of causing damage to another person or to the State, uses another person's identification document shall receive a prison sentence of up to 1 year or a fine. CHAPTER II FALSIFICATION OF CURRENCY

Article 307 Counterfeiting of currency

1. A person who counterfeits currency or depreciates legitimate metallic currency, with the intention of putting it into circulation as genuine, shall receive a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years. 2. When, together with the facts described in the previous number, the agent also puts that currency into circulation, the penalty's maximum limit shall be aggravated by one third. 3. A person who, according to an agreement with the falsifier, puts up for people to buy, puts into circulation or, by other means, disseminates that currency shall receive a prison sentence of 3 to 10 years. Article 308 Passing of false currency

Whoever, in cases not falling within the previous article, acquires to put into circulation or indeed puts into circulation, sells or, by any means, disseminates counterfeited or depreciated currency, as genuine currency, shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years. Article 309
Counterfeiting of sealed or stamped value

1. A person who commits counterfeiting or falsification of sealed or stamped value that only the Timorese State can manufacture or supply, to sell, use or, in another way, put into circulation as legitimate, shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 8 years. 2. A person who commits these described acts concerning a postal stamp or other means of postal franking

that Timor-Leste's post office uses shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 3. A person who uses the falsified products that the previous numbers concern, even knowing that these products are falsified, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 2 years or a fine. 4. Attempt gives rise to punishment. Article 310
Counterfeiting of seal, stamp, mark or cancellation

1. A person who, with the intention of using as authentic or intact, acquires, counterfeits151 or falsifies some authority or public department's seal, stamp, mark or cancellation shall receive a prison sentence of 2 to 6 years. 2. Whoever uses the objects that the previous number concerns, when they know that the thing is falsified or lacks authorization from the person who has the right to give it, shall receive a prison sentence of up to 3 years or a fine. 3. When the falsifier themselves uses the objects already concerned, the penalty referred to in number 1's maximum limit shall be aggravated by one third. 4. Attempt concerning the facts that number 2 describes gives rise to punishment. Article 311 Weight and measure

1.1. Any person who, with the intent to harm another person or the State, counterfeits or, by any means, alters, or, having committed such acts, uses weights, measures, scales or other measuring instruments shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Attempt shall give rise to punishment. Article 312 Seizure and forfeiture

Counterfeit, falsified or altered currency and equivalent objects

151 (310) Counterfeit (v) – To make something according to an original in order to sell or give to another person as original; to make something false. and also weights, measures or instruments intended to be used to commit the crimes provided for in this title shall be seized and rendered unusable or destroyed. TITLE VIII
CRIMES AGAINST THE ECONOMY

Article 313 Money laundering

1. Any person who, knowing that any property or proceeds derive from the crime of terrorism, trafficking in arms or nuclear products, trafficking or sale of persons, pornography with minors, corruption, fraud or extortion, fiscal fraud, trafficking in protected species and trafficking in human organs or tissues or any other serious crime, which any person has committed, with any form of participation, and giving rise to a penalty with a minimum limit higher than 4 years' imprisonment:
a) Converts, transfers, assists or facilitates any operation to convert or transfer such property or proceeds, wholly or partially, directly or indirectly, in order to conceal or disguise the illicit origin of such proceeds or to assist any person implicated in any of these infractions to evade the legal consequences arising from his or her conduct; b) Conceals152 or disguises153 the nature, origin, location, disposition, movement or ownership of or rights concerning such property or proceeds; c) Acquires or receives such advantage under any title or uses, holds or preserves such advantage,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 4 to 12 years. 2. The penalties for the acts set out in the preceding number, subparagraphs a) to c), shall continue to apply even if the agent committed the act constituting the predicate crime154 in non-national territory or does not know where the crime was committed or whether the crime was actually committed. 152 (313) Conceal (v) – To hide. 153 (313) Disguise (v) – To pretend in order to hide
154 (313) Predicate crime – The crime from which the property, proceeds or right to be laundered derives. 3. The knowledge, intent or purpose that must enter as a constitutive element of the crime may be recognized from the effective and concrete factual circumstances. 4. There need not be a prior conviction for the commission of the predicate crime in order to demonstrate and prove that the proceeds have an illicit origin. 5. The predicate infraction also includes crimes which the agent committed in non-national territory when such acts constitute a crime in the State where the agent committed the crime and in the territory of Timor-Leste. 6. The crime of money laundering does not apply to the person who committed the predicate crime. 7. Attempt at money laundering gives rise to conviction and may also give rise to a reduction in penalty as a general rule. 8. The penalty provided for the typical illicit acts described in number 1, subparagraphs a) to c) may not be higher than the maximum limit of the penalty provided for the corresponding predicate infraction. Article 314 Fiscal fraud

1. Any person who, in order not to pay or to cause another not to pay, wholly or partially, any tax, fee or pecuniary fiscal obligation that must be paid to the State, by reason of
a) Not declaring facts giving rise to taxation or which are necessary to make the assessment; b) Incorrectly155 declaring facts which serve as the basis for taxation; c) Preventing by any means or withholding156 elements necessary to carry out inspection of the activity or facts giving rise to taxation,
shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years. 2. When the amount due that has not yet been paid is higher than 5,000 US dollars the agent shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 8 years. 155 (314) Incorrectly (adv) – Wrongly. To declare incorrectly = to declare wrongly; not to declare truthfully. 156 (314) Withhold (v) – Not to include or describe (something) in a situation where, according to law, one must include or describe; to hide. Article 315
Illicit importation and exportation of goods or merchandise

1. Any person who exports or imports goods or merchandise which, according to law, must have a license from any entity in order to import or export, without having a license, or not passing through customs shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 2. When the agent commits the act through negligence a penalty of imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine shall be applied. Article 316 Smuggling

1. Any person who does not pass through customs or through other mandatory control goods or merchandise entering or leaving the territory of Timor-Leste which he or she imports or exports shall be punished with imprisonment from 2 to 6 years or a fine. 2. When the goods or merchandise have a value higher than 10,000 US dollars or the agent habitually commits the acts described in the preceding number a penalty of imprisonment from 2 to 8 years or a fine shall be applied. 3. When the conduct referred to in the preceding numbers concerns goods or merchandise which must have a license to import or export or whose importation or exportation is prohibited by law, the minimum and maximum limits of the penalty referred to in the preceding numbers shall be aggravated by one sixth. Article 317 Evasion of duties

1. Any person who imports or exports goods or merchandise but evades paying wholly or partially the duties or customs fees which he or she must pay by reason of the entry or exit of such merchandise shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 4 years or a fine. 2. When the goods or merchandise have a value higher than 10,000 US dollars or the agent habitually commits the acts described in the preceding number a penalty of imprisonment from 2 to 6 years or a fine shall be applied. Article 318 Exemption from penalty

An agent who commits the acts described in the preceding articles may be granted exemption from penalty when he or she voluntarily pays the duties or customs fees to be assessed and his or her conduct is only occasional157. Article 319 Irregular administration of public funds

1. Any person who gives public funds an application which is not the same as that which the law establishes shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 2 years or a fine. 2. When such irregular application is not made for a public purpose which the law establishes a penalty of imprisonment from 2 to 6 years shall be applied. Article 320
Disobedience to requisition of goods

1. Any person who does not comply with a requisition of goods considered indispensable to establish economic activity or public consumption, as ordered by the Government, shall be punished with imprisonment from 1 to 4 years or a fine. 2. Negligent conduct shall give rise to a penalty of imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine. Article 321
Destruction of goods relevant to the economy

1. Any person who, by any means, destroys, damages or renders unusable goods which have a relevant interest for the national economy or, in any way, removes such goods from the legal obligation which must exist by reason of

157 (318) Occasional (adj) – Which happens by reason of some circumstance; accidental; eventual; casual; fortuitous; unforeseen. Occasional conduct = conduct which arises by reason of some circumstance, but in a normal situation does not happen. the interest of the national economy shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Negligent conduct shall give rise to a penalty of imprisonment for up to 1 year or a fine. Article 322 Exploitation of illicit gambling

1. Any person who, in any way, exploits games of fortune or chance in places other than those which the law authorizes and without due legal authorization or ensures158 luck through error, deceit or any equipment, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 2. Games of fortune or chance are those whose result is uncertain because they are based only or mainly on luck. Article 323 Disturbance of public acts

Any person who, with the intent to prevent or prejudice the result of a judicial auction or with respect to a judicial auction or public tender, manages, through gift, promise, violence or threat, to cause any person not to submit a bid or not to compete or, even if submitting a bid and auction, such person does not carry out such acts with freedom, shall be punished with imprisonment for up to 3 years or a fine. 158 (322) Ensure (v) – To hold firmly, to guarantee. Law 13/2008, of 13 October Legislative authorization in penal matters

The constitutional mechanism concerning legislative authorization is set out in article 96, no. 1, subparagraph a), of the Constitution of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste. This constitutional mechanism makes the legislative process proceed more quickly: Parliament defines the general orientation of legislative policy which frames the definitive diploma; the Government shall harmonize each aspect concerning legal technique, according to the orientation coming from the National Parliament. Thus the separation in the competence of each constitutional organ to exercise legislative power shall continue to be followed. This law for legislative authorization fixes its content and exact extension in order to guarantee respect for the fundamental rights, freedoms and guarantees of citizens in the area of Criminal Law. The solutions presented also take into account the general principles which the international legal instruments which Timor-Leste has ratified consecrate, and also the social and cultural context of the country. The content of this legislative authorization also articulates with the Code of Criminal Procedure and complementary legislation which has already been approved and is in force. With this approval Indonesian Criminal Code shall no longer be applied subsidiarily in Timor-Leste and legislative diplomas which came into force after 20 May 2002 and have any norm which defines crimes, penalties or security measures shall be revoked or harmonized. Thus, based on articles 95, no. 1, and 96 of the Constitution, the National Parliament decrees, to be valid as law, the following:

Article 1 Object
