Legislative authorization is given to the Government to approve the Criminal Code and revoke legislation in force in this matter, namely concerning the definition of crimes, penalties and security measures and their presuppositions. Article 2 Meaning and extension

1. The Criminal Code elaborated on the basis of this legislative authorization shall follow the constitutional principles and norms and also the norms in the international instruments which bind the Timorese State in this matter. 2. The meaning and extension of this authorization are as follows:
a) To establish a legal-penal system which protects legal goods essential to life in Timorese society and reintegrates the delinquent into society; b) To consecrate the principle of legality and the prohibition of analogy concerning the application of penal law, but with the possibility of applying the law which, after the commission of the crime, comes into force when, in a concrete situation, it favors the agent; c) To stipulate that exceptional or temporary law shall apply to acts committed during the time such law is in force even if the judgment is made at a time when such law is no longer in force; d) To establish norms which define the place and moment at which a person commits a crime; e) To consecrate the principle of territoriality in the application of Timorese penal law, indicating each limitation on this principle and the situations in which such law applies in non-Timorese territory; f) To regulate the general presuppositions of crime and, in this, to equate omission with action, except when the law has another intent, when any result enters into the legal type of crime and the agent has an obligation to avoid such result; g) To establish that, in order to have objective imputation, there must be a causal nexus between conduct and typical result; h) In order to have objective imputation there must be intent or negligence, and the various types of intent (direct, necessary and eventual) and the various types of negligence (conscious and unconscious) shall be defined; i) To provide that criminal responsibility for crimes provided for in the Criminal Code may not be transmitted and only singular persons are its agents, but also to admit that there may be responsibility for acting in the name of another person and special legislation may consecrate the criminal responsibility of collective persons; j) To consecrate the inimputability of minors under 16 years of age and also the requirements for inimputability by reason of psychic anomaly; k) To admit that the penalty may be aggravated when the agent's responsibility, at least for negligence, must embrace a result which does not enter into the type; l) To consecrate error concerning circumstances as a basis for removing intent and error concerning illicitness as a basis for removing culpability; m) Concerning the various forms of crime, to establish as a principle that preparatory acts may not give rise to punishment, and also rules concerning the punishment of attempt in intentional crimes which give rise to imprisonment higher than 3 years; n) To define the concept of attempt, the punishment of attempt up to where, cases in which there is impossible attempt, voluntary desistance and subsequent repentance, in singular action or in participation; o) To establish the various forms of authorship, instigation or complicity, and to define the responsibility of participants in culpability and illicitness; p) To provide for and define presuppositions for situations in which many crimes enter into concurrence, many sanctions enter into concurrence, crimes enter into concurrence with another infraction and many norms enter into concurrence; q) To establish rules concerning punishment for crimes which enter into concurrence, even with supervenient knowledge, and also to admit that cumulation may give rise to imprisonment for up to 30 years and a fine for up to 600 days; r) To define continued crime and rules concerning its punishment with the penalty applicable to the most serious conduct; s) To consecrate causes which exclude illicitness and culpability, and to autonomize them systematically and define the presuppositions for the application of each; t) To include in one title the various circumstances which, even if they do not enter into the legal type, have relevance to determine the agent's criminal responsibility; u) To enumerate159 general aggravating circumstances and also general attenuating circumstances; v) To provide for a general clause concerning extraordinary attenuation, together with special cases provided for in the law and to delimit their consequences in the determination of the penal framework, with a model which includes various degrees of attenuation; w) As general modificative aggravating circumstances, to consecrate recidivism and criminal habituality with their presuppositions and consequences for the penalty; x) To establish a system concerning penalties and security measures and to base their difference on whether the agent who committed the act has capacity or not to receive culpability; in the first case the concrete penalty may not be more serious than the agent's culpability and in the second case it must be based on the agent's dangerousness; y) To enunciate principles determinative160 for choosing the penalty and security measure and also determinative concerning the effect of penalties and security measures and concerning the counting of time in which there is deprivation of liberty before conviction; z) Concerning the execution of penalties and measures privative of liberty, to provide for the possibility of granting conditional release and probation regime, when, with the consent of the convict and with other requirements which shall be set out in complementary legislation which shall provide for conditions

159 Enumerate (v) – To make a list. To enumerate aggravating circumstances = To include in a list the aggravating circumstances. 160 Determinative (adj) – Fundamental; which serves as a basis; which serves as a foundation; which has great influence. for the execution of imprisonment, the rights and obligations of prisoners and also open regime; aa) To establish a system of criminal sanctions for imputables based on the following principal penalties: imprisonment, fine, community service and admonishment; bb) Imprisonment with its minimum duration of 30 days and maximum of 30 years, with the possibility of suspending its execution when the penalty applied is not longer than 3 years and the fulfillment of such penalty is not necessary to prevent future crime, with or without conditions to fulfill any duty and/or to enter into accompaniment for reinsertion during the period of suspension; cc) To regulate the possibility of modifying the suspension regime which is newly applied and the revocation of suspension when during the period of suspension the convict commits an intentional crime which gives rise to him or her receiving a penalty of imprisonment or in the case where there is intentional non-fulfillment of the obligation which falls upon him or her; dd) To consecrate a regime concerning the substitution of imprisonment applied which is not longer than 12 months with a fine or community service, according to each circumstance, with an obligation on the court to justify non-substitution in situations where, even if possible, this measure is not applied; ee) Not to give correspondence between alternative imprisonment and substitutive fine and, when there is unjustified non-payment of such fine, to give an obligation to apply imprisonment which is newly applied; ff) To stipulate that the fine penalty must be fixed according to time, with its minimum of 10 days and its maximum of 360 days, as a rule, and to determine that each day of fine shall have correspondence with an amount, with its minimum of half a dollar and its maximum of 200 dollars, which shall be fixed according to the convict's economic and financial situation; gg) When directly applying a fine penalty, to stipulate an obligation to give correspondence to such fine with alternative imprisonment for a corresponding time reduced by 2/3, and also the possibility of paying the fine by installment, reduction or exemption of such fine in cases

where there is great supervenient difficulty or impossibility of paying such fine; hh) To provide for the possibility of placing a penalty of community service in place of a penalty of imprisonment or a penalty of fine in situations concerning petty criminality and medium criminality and to establish conditions for its application and execution according to coordination with reinsertion services, and also cases concerning exemption or reduction, and to leave to complementary law to regulate other conditions concerning the application and functioning of this penalty; ii) To consecrate the penalty of admonishment for cases not punishable with abstract imprisonment longer than 3 years, namely, cumulatively, where there is reparation for damage, the agent is a primary delinquent and admonishment is sufficient to make prevention and recovery; jj) To establish a system of accessory penalties, in which, by reason of certain crimes, rights and the exercise of professions shall be prohibited, namely, temporary suspension from public function, prohibition from exercising functions, prohibition from driving, cancellation of license to possess firearms and expulsion of foreigners; these accessory penalties do not function automatically but are applied only when a principal penalty is applied and their measure varies according to the measure of culpability; kk) To consecrate for those who have already been declared inimputable by reason of psychic anomaly the possibility of applying a security measure, with limited temporal duration, namely internment when dangerousness counsels it and also to consecrate that the obligation to apply such measure ends when the state which gave rise to such measure ends, and for foreigners expulsion may be placed in place of such measure; ll) To provide also for inimputables by reason of psychic anomaly measures of professional interdiction, cancellation of license and prohibition from driving; mm) To establish an autonomous chapter concerning the determination of concrete penalty, for imprisonment and for fine, indicating general principles and operations necessary for the choice and quantification of such penalty; nn) To establish that civil law regulates compensation for loss and damage arising from the commission of crime, and also its presuppositions and calculation, but, except for another declaration by the injured party, the court shall award compensation ex officio and the credit arising from this shall have privilege; oo) To determine conditions which give rise to the court declaring forfeited objects or advantages arising from crime; pp) To define, as a criterion for exercising the right to complaint, the various crimes as public or semi-public, to identify the holder of this right, the time limit for exercising this right and conditions for renunciation and desistance of this right; qq) Concerning the extinction of criminal responsibility, to fix the time limit for the prescription of criminal proceedings, taking into account the various frameworks of the various crimes, how to count and causes for the suspension of prescription; rr) To fix the time limit for the prescription of penalties, security measures and accessory penalties, and also situations which give rise to the suspension of prescription; ss) To regulate other extinctions of responsibility, by reason of the agent's death, by reason of amnesty, by reason of generic pardon and by reason of pardon; tt) To consecrate imprescriptibility for criminal proceedings and penalties for war crimes, crimes against peace, humanity and freedom; uu) To organize the special part of the Criminal Code, book II, in which the various types of crime are included according to the legal good protected in titles and chapters as homogeneous as possible; vv) In title I to include illicit acts concerning the crime of genocide and crimes against humanity, war crimes, including prohibited conducts which use methods and means referred to in international law, war crimes against property and against goods protected with distinctive insignia or emblem or other rights, and, also in this title, the crime called crime against peace and freedom, namely to prohibit incitement to war, racially or religiously discriminatory conduct, trafficking and enslavement

of persons and also to consecrate a type of crime concerning terrorism; ww) In title I of the general part the future Criminal Code of Timor-Leste must also pay attention to the international obligations which the Country has already accepted when it subscribed to and ratified the Statute of the International Criminal Court; xx) In title II of the special part legal goods which are eminently personal shall be protected, such as life, physical integrity, personal freedom, honor and protection of private life; yy) To structure protection of the legal good life with provision for various penal frameworks according to what type of culpability the agent has (intent and negligence) and circumstances which qualify the degree of censurability as lesser or greater, namely according to its motive, the quality of the victim and the means and method used to realize the criminal design; zz) To consecrate protection of uterine life; to provide as privileged the conduct of an infanticidal parturient in limited cases which happen during childbirth by reason of the disturbing influence of childbirth; to criminalize incitement to suicide and abandonment or intentional exposure of another person which creates danger to life; aaa) To define a broad incriminating norm which prohibits conduct violating the physical integrity of another person, with graduation of the abstract penal framework according to the agent's illicitness and culpability which is indicated in the gravity of the injury, according to the type of culpability and according to whether the result which exceeds the criminal design formulated may give rise to imputation or not, at least for negligence - what doctrine calls aggravation by result; bbb) To autonomize incrimination for offense with poisonous substance, reciprocal offense and participation in a fight with two or more persons; ccc) In the context of bodily offense, to implement two legal types of crime which provide for punishment for ill-treatment of minors or incapables and for spouse or person who lives as husband or wife; ddd) In the chapter concerning personal freedom, conduct which, traditionally and according to comparative law, integrates the types of crime of threat, coercion, kidnapping, abduction, and other acts which violate individual freedom and, specifically, to create a typical illicit act concerning trafficking in persons and omission of denunciation with respect to situations which may constitute these crimes; eee) To consecrate a chapter on sexual criminality, with emphasis on the legal good of freedom in this area as determinative for the conduct to be criminalized, in order to protect those who are still younger from acts for which they do not yet have preparation to, according to their conscience, decide with freedom, to incriminate conduct which constitutes the most common forms of sexual exploitation and sexual abuse, such as with fraud or by reason of age; fff) To incriminate especially practices which constitute sexual prostitution, child pornography and other sexual acts with minors under 12 years of age; ggg) To include criminalization for violation of secrecy, violation of domicile and other places and violation of correspondence and telecommunications; hhh) To bring together in one title only the crimes which protect legal goods concerning public peace and tranquility, the security of the State, life in society, against public authority and in the context of electoral activity; iii) Concerning conduct which may violate public peace and tranquility, to prevent criminal association, public instigation to commit crime, participation in public riot, conduct which prevents citizens from exercising political rights, disobedience to legitimate order to disperse, to criminalize practices which constitute trafficking in influence and also to punish abuse of public signs or uniforms; jjj) Concerning the internal security of the State, to provide for incriminating norms for conduct which may actually create danger to such security, namely to consecrate as legal types of crime treason to the fatherland, collaboration with enemy armed forces of the Timorese State, sabotage of national defense and campaign against efforts

for peace in Timor-Leste, attack against the Head of State or other constitutional organs or conduct which seeks to alter the State according to law, diplomatic infidelity161, violation of State secrecy and outrage162 to Timorese national symbols; kkk) To criminalize fraudulent conduct in electoral activity independently of its agent and the phase of the electoral process, obstruction of exercising candidacy or illegal exercise of candidacy, electoral propaganda against the respective legal framework163 and actions which disturb the normal development of electoral acts; lll) Life in modern society develops together with conduct which may give rise to danger with more or less proximate concretization and may violate goods whose integrity must be guaranteed, such as driving motor vehicles, using or possessing weapons and activity with relevance to citizens and public health; therefore some types of crime concerning this matter must be provided for; mmm) Thus also conduct which violates religious values implanted in Timorese society, non-fulfillment of alimentary obligation and other obligations concerning the custody of minors, of solidarity in calamity or disaster or obligations which fall upon certain professional activities must be criminalized; nnn) To criminally sanction conduct harmful164 and unsustainable to the environment, namely fauna, flora, natural habitat and resources in the Timorese subsoil; ooo) To criminally sanction conduct against the exercise of public authority, namely with acts which constitute disobedience, obstruction, destruction, resistance or usurpation of public office; ppp) Concerning the executing action of penalties and security measures which remove liberty, to criminalize conduct which constitutes removal of prisoner, evasion,

161 Infidelity (s) – Treason; violation of trust
162 Outrage (s) – Offense; fault. 163 Framework (s) – Disposition. Legal framework = legal disposition; norm. 164 Harmful (adj) – Which harms or creates prejudice

assistance by an official for evasion and riot of prisoners; qqq) In order to protect patrimonial legal goods to consecrate in one chapter crimes against property and in another chapter crimes against patrimony; rrr) To establish a normative system protective of property based on the legal types of crime which have long been used in other penal legislation, such as theft, robbery, breach of trust and damage, and to structure these types as simple and aggravated, according to circumstances such as the value and nature of the thing appropriated, the means used, how the action is, violence, threat or coercion or other circumstances which may significantly increase culpability and illicitness; sss) Concerning property, to also autonomize norms which typify theft for use of vehicle, violence following subtraction, usurpation of immovable and alteration of boundary marker, also to incriminate involuntary damage and to define prohibited burning and types of arson as typical crimes concerning special damage by reason of the means and danger which it represents with the consequences which arise from such conduct; ttt) To protect patrimony in general with the following common incriminations, such as fraud, extortion, receiving and bankruptcy or insolvency, but especially computer fraud and various harmful and abusive administration, looking to new needs which arise in commerce with new technology and looking to the responsibility which must be demanded from persons who manage the goods or patrimony of another person and do not act correctly in such administration; uuu) In modern and democratic society justice and the exercise of justice are also goods which must receive penal protection; thus punishment must be consecrated for falsity of procedural acts, for various obstructions to jurisdictional activity, non-exercise of jurisdictional activity when it constitutes denial of justice and violation of the secrecy of justice; vvv) To also include incrimination for acts which constitute bribery, prevarication of magistrate or official and lawyer or public defender, and also other activity concerning personal favoritism in justice, together

with classic incriminations such as calumnious denunciation, simulation of crime or non-participation; www) Together with the references set out in the last two numbers, conduct must also be criminalized which, in the exercise of public function, constitutes corruption, peculation, abuse of power or public force or economic participation in business by persons who exercise public office or function; xxx) In one autonomous title conduct which constitutes falsification of document, technical notation, currency and sealed or stamped value, weights and measures, marks, stamps and seals must be prevented with adequate incriminating norms, with their various sanctions according to the nature, probative or fiduciary value and public use or destination of the falsified object, and to provide for the possibility of seizure and forfeiture of objects intended to commit these crimes; yyy) In the area of the economy acts which constitute fiscal fraud or smuggling and evasion of duties concerning customs or border matters shall also be incriminated, together with criminal punishment for disobedience to requisition which the government orders and conduct which may disturb, prejudice or prevent the realization of any public act, such as public tender or judicial auction. Article 3
Articulation with the Code of Criminal Procedure

The Criminal Code which this legislative authorization refers to must articulate its moment of coming into force with the Code of Criminal Procedure and regulatory legislation concerning penal matters which is already in force, in order to guarantee legal security and coherence in the respective legal order. Article 4 Duration and extension

The legislative authorization given by this law is in force for 180 days, beginning at the moment this law comes into force. Article 5 Coming into force

This legal diploma comes into force on the day following its publication. Approved on 23 September 2008

President of the National Parliament in exercise Maria da Paixão de Jesus da Costa

Promulgated on 7 October 2008 Let it be published. President of the Republic Dr. José Ramos Horta

Law 6/2009, of 15 July (First amendment, as parliamentary appreciation, to the Criminal Code approved by Decree-Law 19/2009, of 8 April)

Life must receive protection beginning immediately from the conception of the human being. Having accepted this principle, we cannot forget that there are also situations which may justify interruption of pregnancy. However, because the goods which enter into conflict here are very serious, the life of the pregnant woman and the life of the fetus or embryo, both with equal value only, only in extreme situations may we consider such interruption legitimate: when with the sacrifice of one life one may save another life and it is not possible to preserve both lives at once. Thus, it must be established that interruption of pregnancy may exist only when with such interruption the pregnant woman does not die, and it is the woman who shall decide, according to her conscience, concerning such interruption. Thus, according to article 92, no.1, and 98, the National Parliament decrees, to have the force of law, the following:

Article 1 Amendment to the Penal Code

Article 141 of the Penal Code approved by Decree-Law 19/2009, of 8 April, and annexed to that diploma, is amended as follows:

"Article 141 (…)

1. (…)
2. (…)
3. (…)
4. The provision of the preceding number does not apply when, according to existing medical knowledge and experience and having done everything that can be done to save the pregnant woman and

the fetus or embryo's life, the interruption of pregnancy is what can avert death from the pregnant woman, namely with medical certification and performed by a doctor or other health professional, under the direction of a doctor, in a public health establishment or officially recognized, and with the pregnant woman's consent. 5. Before performing the interruption a panel of three doctors must certify the circumstances referred to in the preceding number, in a written and signed medical certificate; in that panel shall be included the doctor who performs or supervises the performance of the interruption of pregnancy. 6. The consent of the pregnant woman shall be given personally in a written and signed document, having heard, when possible, her spouse, or the person with whom she lives as if married, or another person, at the request of that woman, who shall give it, and, when possible, shall be given at least two days before performing that interruption. 7. When the pregnant woman is a minor, her legal representative shall give consent. 8. When the pregnant woman is an adult or emancipated person who is psychically incapable or a person who is suffering from psychic incapacity, her spouse or the person with whom she lives as if married, or her legal representative, ascendant or descendant, or, if there is none, any collateral relative, respectively and successively, shall give that consent. 9. When the medical certificate referred to in no. 5 cannot be obtained and/or the consent as written in
nos. 6 to 8 cannot be obtained, and the interruption must be performed urgently and cannot be delayed any longer, the doctor, having regard to each situation, shall decide, also using, when possible, the opinion of other doctors. 10. In the panel referred to in no. 5 shall be included a doctor who has adequate knowledge to evaluate the circumstances that justify the interruption of pregnancy, and, when possible, a gynecologist/obstetrician. 11. The doctors who issue the medical certificate and the doctors who perform or supervise the performance of the interruption of pregnancy must provide the necessary clarifications to the pregnant woman and, when it falls within the situation, to the persons whom nos. 7 and 8 refer to, namely concerning the method to be used in the interruption, the interruption

itself and its effects and its consequences for the pregnant woman's physical and psychic health. 12. Doctors and other health professionals have the right to present conscientious objection to any act concerning the interruption of pregnancy. 13. Doctors and other health professionals who invoke conscientious objection must guarantee the immediate intervention of another health professional in the acts that must be performed and also the immediate accompaniment of the pregnant woman. 14. Conscientious objection must be expressed explicitly and must be made known to the clinical head in the health establishment where the objector works". Article 2 Entry into force

This diploma enters into force on the day following its publication. Approved on 26 May 2009

President of the National Parliament Fernando La Sama de Araújo

Promulgated on 3 July 2009 Published by

President of the Republic José Ramos Horta
